Diagnosed model hallucination loop and engineered protective safeguards.

Diagnosed model hallucination loop and engineered protective safeguards.

Done

This is a clean, textbook case of seq2seq degenerate repetition — worth naming precisely, because it points straight at the fix. Look at what happened: the raw OCR line (6৭51581928 25015 ০£ 20৩00 ) is genuinely garbage (that's Tesseract force-mapping "equivalent inches of mercury" onto Bangla digit-glyphs, same failure family we discussed with ben-only forcing English into fake Bangla). The corrector has never seen anything like that in training, so it has no real "correction" to make — but nothing in correct_batch() stops it from generating anyway, and with greedy decoding (num_beams=1, no repetition guard) a confused model very commonly gets stuck in a token loop once it starts, which is exactly the 'CaCO', 'CaCO', 'CaCO'... you're seeing until it hits max_length. This isn't really a "wrong correction" — it's the model getting stuck, and it's actively worse than doing nothing, because it hallucinates fake plausible-Bangla chemistry vocabulary into a citation line.

Two separate, complementary fixes — one prevents feeding garbage in, one prevents the model from spiraling even when it does get bad input:

1. Don't send unrecoverable garbled lines to the corrector at all

Since your current ocr.py doesn't have the word-level dual-lang logic we built earlier, the simplest fit here is a cheap pre-filter: if a line is mostly non-Bangla characters (digits, symbols, Latin punctuation — the signature of force-mapped English/citations), skip correction and keep the raw Tesseract output. Raw garbage is bad, but bounded garbage — a hallucination loop is unbounded and actively worse for anything downstream (chunking, embedding).

# vision/ocr.py — add near _normalize()

_BANGLA_RANGE = (0x0980, 0x09FF)

def _bangla_char_ratio(text: str) -> float:
    letters = [ch for ch in text if ch.isalpha() or (0x0980 <= ord(ch) <= 0x09FF)]
    if not letters:
        return 1.0  # nothing to judge (pure punctuation/numbers) — don't block correction on this alone
    bangla = sum(1 for ch in letters if _BANGLA_RANGE[0] <= ord(ch) <= _BANGLA_RANGE[1])
    return bangla / len(letters)

In the Pass 2 setup, split lines into "safe to correct" vs "leave alone" before calling the corrector:

# replace the flat_lines collection in Pass 1's inner loop with a filtered version,
# or filter right before Pass 2:

bangla_ratio_thr = float(ocr_cfg.get("min_bangla_ratio_for_correction", 0.6))
correctable_idx = [i for i, ln in enumerate(flat_lines) if _bangla_char_ratio(ln) >= bangla_ratio_thr]
correctable_lines = [flat_lines[i] for i in correctable_idx]

corrected_subset = _get_corrector(cfg).correct_batch(correctable_lines, batch_size=batch_size) \
    if use_corrector and correctable_lines else []

corrected_flat = list(flat_lines)  # default: keep raw for lines we didn't send
for i, corrected in zip(correctable_idx, corrected_subset):
    corrected_flat[i] = corrected

This means your citation lines ([ L. S. S. KUMAR ... ], The Indian Cotton Atlas..., and this new "equivalent inches of mercury" case) stay as raw Tesseract output — imperfect, but not actively corrupted into fake chemistry terms.

2. Guard the generation itself, so even a genuinely Bangla-heavy but confusing line can't spiral

This is the more important structural fix, since filter #1 is a heuristic and won't catch every bad case. Add real repetition controls to model.generate():

# vision/ocr.py — in _Corrector.correct_batch()

gen_ids = self.model.generate(
    **inputs,
    max_length=self.max_length,
    num_beams=1,
    repetition_penalty=1.3,       # penalize repeating the same token
    no_repeat_ngram_size=3,       # hard-block any 3-gram from repeating verbatim
    early_stopping=True,
)

And add a post-generation sanity check as a last line of defense — if the model's output is still degenerate despite the guards above, fall back to the original line rather than accept it:

# vision/ocr.py — new helper

def _is_degenerate(text: str, max_word_repeat: int = 4) -> bool:
    """Catch outputs stuck repeating the same short token/word run --
    the seq2seq-repetition failure mode, not a real correction."""
    words = text.split()
    if len(words) < max_word_repeat:
        return False
    for i in range(len(words) - max_word_repeat + 1):
        window = words[i:i + max_word_repeat]
        if len(set(window)) == 1:  # same word repeated max_word_repeat times in a row
            return True
    return False

Apply it right after decoding, inside correct_batch:

decoded = self.tokenizer.batch_decode(gen_ids, skip_special_tokens=True)
decoded = [orig if _is_degenerate(corr) else corr for orig, corr in zip(batch, decoded)]
out.extend(decoded)

Net effect and how to verify it

Together: garbled non-Bangla content skips correction entirely (bounded, honest raw output), and any Bangla-ish line that still confuses the model gets caught by the repetition guard and reverts to raw rather than shipping a hallucination loop. Rerun your held-out CER/WER on the same sample — I'd expect this to actually reduce CER/WER versus your original run, since a 'CaCO'-loop line contributes enormous edit distance against the real ground truth, far worse than the original garbled-but-bounded raw text would have.

Worth flagging as a known, documented limitation in design_choices.md rather than something to keep chasing: citation lines mixing Bangla with English source attributions and technical Latin terms are a recurring OCR weak point in this corpus (you've now seen this pattern at least three times — the plant-usage table, the "[L. S. S. KUMAR...]" captions, and this rainfall map citation). That's a legitimate, honest thing to name as a corpus-specific edge case your pipeline handles gracefully (falls back to raw, doesn't hallucinate) rather than solves perfectly — which is a defensible scope call given your robust-and-reliable NFR is about not confidently returning wrong answers, and "admit uncertainty on this citation line" is exactly that principle in action.

Depends on where your numbers actually stand and how much runway you have left — worth a quick gut-check before sinking more time in.

Signal to check first

You've made three real, structural OCR improvements now: word-level dual-lang resolution, the correction pre-filter, and the repetition guard. Rerun your held-out CER/WER (Krishi-Darpan) on a representative sample and look at where you land:

If CER is roughly in the 5–15% range, that's already a defensible, gradeable number for a degraded-scan Bangla corpus — genuinely good OCR on modern clean scans is often 2–5%; your corpus is 90+ years old with matra-heavy conjuncts, so expectations should be calibrated accordingly. At that point, further OCR tuning has steeply diminishing returns relative to time spent.

If it's still 25%+, there's likely a bigger structural issue left (e.g. a preprocessing/binarization problem affecting many pages, not something the correction layer can fix) — that's worth one more targeted look, but a different kind of fix, not more corrector tuning.

Why I'd lean toward stopping soon regardless

A few things point toward moving on rather than continuing to chase OCR:

You have a downstream reranker and retrieval pipeline now built specifically to be tolerant of imperfect chunk text. Retrieval doesn't need character-perfect OCR — it needs the gist of a passage to be recoverable, which dense embeddings are fairly robust to even with moderate character-level noise. Diminishing OCR returns matter less than they would if this were, say, a pure transcription deliverable.

You still have real, ungated stages ahead: retrieval/retriever.py (wiring the reranker in), agent/agent.py (the actual evidence-gated abstention logic your NFR is built around), and the eval harness tying it together in kb_demo.ipynb. Given your primary NFR is robust-and-reliable with evidence-gated abstention, that agent-level behavior is arguably a bigger grading lever than shaving another 2 points off CER — an agent that correctly says "I don't have confident evidence for this" when retrieval quality is marginal is precisely what your NFR is asking for, and it's not yet built.

The remaining OCR failure mode is a known, narrow edge case (mixed Bangla/English/Latin citation lines) that you've now handled gracefully (falls back to raw rather than hallucinating) even if not perfectly. That's a legitimate, already-documented stopping point for design_choices.md — "further OCR gains would require citation-specific handling, deprioritized in favor of retrieval/agent work" is a defensible scope call, not an admission of giving up.

One cheap thing worth doing before moving on

Run the corpus-wide (not just held-out) CER/WER once, and eyeball 5-10 random chunks side by side with their source page images — not to fix anything, just to confirm there's no other systemic issue (like a preprocessing step silently corrupting a subset of pages) hiding behind an okay-looking aggregate number. That's a 15-minute sanity check, cheap insurance before you commit to moving forward, versus open-ended OCR tuning with unclear payoff.

If your numbers come back healthy, I'd move to retrieval/retriever.py next — want to start there?

Done

Your numbers are actually reasonable for this corpus — CER around 11-13% is solid for 90-year-old degraded Bangla scans, and the corrector is doing real work (WER dropped from 39% to 27%). The gap between CER (11%) and WER (27-29%) is itself informative: relatively few character-level errors are landing on enough different words to disproportionately inflate WER — which lines up exactly with your English-garbling theory, since a single garbled English span (like the "equivalent inches of mercury" citation) can wreck several whole words' worth of WER while only contributing a modest character count.

Worth noting: the ocr.py you just pasted doesn't include the word-level dual-lang resolution we built together a few messages back (_resolve_mixed_script_line, _word_boxes, the per-word confidence gate) — it's back to the plain _tesseract_region(crop, lang=lang, psm=psm) call. If that got dropped or reverted, that's the highest-leverage thing to reinstate before trying anything new, since it directly targets your stated concern (English force-mapped to random Bangla glyphs) rather than being a general accuracy tweak.

Priority order, cheapest/highest-impact first

1. Reinstate the word-level dual-lang gate. This is the direct fix for "English text is randomly mapped" — nothing else on this list addresses that specific failure mode as directly. Since you already have the pre-filter and repetition guard working now, layering the word-level ben/eng per-word resolution back in should compound with those rather than conflict.

2. Try --psm 4 instead of --psm 6 for multi-column or citation-heavy text regions. PSM 6 assumes a single uniform block of text; PSM 4 assumes a single column of variable-sized text, which can help when a region mixes body text with a differently-sized citation line (exactly your [ L. S. S. KUMAR ... ] pattern) — worth an A/B test on a 20-page sample, not a blind swap.

3. Check if --oem 1 (LSTM-only engine) vs. the Tesseract default is actually what you're running. Some Tesseract builds default to a legacy+LSTM combined mode; the pure LSTM engine (--oem 1) is generally more accurate for degraded scans specifically. Quick to test: pytesseract.image_to_string(rgb, lang=lang, config=f"--psm {psm} --oem 1").

4. A user-defined character whitelist/blacklist is not worth pursuing here — Bangla's large glyph set and the legitimate mixed-script content in your corpus make whitelisting counterproductive; skip this one.

5. Diminishing-returns checkpoint still applies. After reinstating word-level dual-lang and testing PSM/OEM variants (cheap, an afternoon's work), I'd stop rather than keep iterating — rerun held-out CER/WER, and if WER lands meaningfully below ~25%, that's a good place to lock OCR and move to retrieval/retriever.py, which is still the bigger lever for your robust-and-reliable NFR.

Want me to give you the merged ocr.py with the word-level dual-lang gate re-added on top of your current pre-filter + repetition-guard version, so you're not reconciling two divergent copies by hand?

8/14/2026 15:03:56

Here's the consolidated file with all three fixes layered in — word-level dual-lang resolution, a Bangla-ratio pre-filter before correction, and a repetition guard on the corrector's output.

Summary of changes:

Added _word_boxes(), _resolve_mixed_script_line() — per-word ben/eng resolution, gated by each word's own Tesseract confidence, with a decisive-margin swap rule.

Added _bangla_char_ratio() — used to skip sending non-Bangla-heavy lines (citations, garbled English) to the corrector at all.

Added _is_degenerate() — catches repetition-loop output from the corrector and falls back to the pre-correction line.

_Corrector.correct_batch() — added repetition_penalty, no_repeat_ngram_size, early_stopping to model.generate(), plus the degenerate-output fallback.

Pass 1 loop — now calls _resolve_mixed_script_line() instead of _tesseract_region() directly (config-gated), and now also accepts oem/psm from config instead of hardcoded PSM-only.

Pass 2 — lines are filtered by _bangla_char_ratio() before correction; non-Bangla-heavy lines bypass the corrector and keep their (already dual-lang-resolved) text as-is.

New config keys, all with safe defaults: dual_lang_word_level, per_word_conf_thr, swap_margin, min_bangla_ratio_for_correction, tesseract_oem.

from __future__ import annotations
"""Stage 3 — OCR/HTR (BASELINE = pretrained TrOCR / Tesseract)

Transcribes page regions detected in Stage 2 using an OCR engine.

Strategy (baseline):
  1. Load OCR model from cfg['ocr']['model'] (e.g., "microsoft/trocr-base-printed")
  2. For each region (except figures), crop the image and run OCR
  3. Return transcribed text as Chunk objects with:
     - text: extracted transcription
     - page_ids: list of pages this chunk came from
     - doc_id: document identifier
  4. Optionally fine-tune on domain-specific data (cfg['ocr']['finetune'])

Reference: EDA §7 "OCR tool bake-off" — tool selection and performance metrics
          EDA §8 "Preprocessing sweep" — per-book preprocessing before OCR
          Config: cfg['ocr']['model'] (HF model ID or path), cfg['ocr']['finetune'] (bool)
"""
"""Stage 3 — OCR/HTR (BASELINE = pretrained foundation, fine-tuned)"""

import re
import time
import unicodedata
from pathlib import Path

import cv2
import numpy as np
import pytesseract
import torch
from jiwer import cer as _cer, wer as _wer
from transformers import AutoModelForSeq2SeqLM, AutoTokenizer
from tqdm import tqdm

from ..contracts import *  # noqa
import json  # add to imports

def _cfg_get(cfg, section, key, default):
    return cfg.get(section, {}).get(key, default)

def _ocr_region_cache_path(ocr_dir: Path, doc_id: str, page_stem: str) -> Path:
    return ocr_dir / "regions" / doc_id / f"{page_stem}.json"

def _save_ocr_regions(path: Path, entries: list[dict]) -> None:
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(entries, f, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2)

def _load_ocr_regions(path: Path) -> list[dict]:
    with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        return json.load(f)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Tesseract reading
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _crop(image_path: str, bbox: tuple[int, int, int, int]) -> np.ndarray:
    img = cv2.imread(image_path)
    if img is None:
        raise ValueError(f"OpenCV could not read {image_path}")
    x1, y1, x2, y2 = bbox
    h, w = img.shape[:2]
    x1, y1 = max(0, x1), max(0, y1)
    x2, y2 = min(w, x2), min(h, y2)
    return img[y1:y2, x1:x2]

def _tesseract_region(img_bgr: np.ndarray, lang: str, psm: int, oem: int = 1) -> str:
    rgb = cv2.cvtColor(img_bgr, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)
    return pytesseract.image_to_string(rgb, lang=lang, config=f"--psm {psm} --oem {oem}").strip()

def _split_lines(text: str) -> list[str]:
    return [ln.strip() for ln in text.splitlines() if ln.strip()]

def _normalize(text: str) -> str:
    text = unicodedata.normalize("NFC", text)
    return re.sub(r"\s+", " ", text).strip()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# NEW: word-level dual-lang (ben/eng) resolution
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_BANGLA_RANGE = (0x0980, 0x09FF)

def _word_boxes(img_bgr: np.ndarray, lang: str, psm: int, oem: int = 1) -> list[dict]:
    """Word-level boxes + text + confidence for one region crop."""
    rgb = cv2.cvtColor(img_bgr, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)
    data = pytesseract.image_to_data(
        rgb, lang=lang, config=f"--psm {psm} --oem {oem}", output_type=pytesseract.Output.DICT
    )
    words = []
    for i, text in enumerate(data["text"]):
        if not text.strip():
            continue
        conf = float(data["conf"][i])
        if conf == -1:
            continue
        words.append({
            "text": text, "conf": conf,
            "left": data["left"][i], "top": data["top"][i],
            "width": data["width"][i], "height": data["height"][i],
            "line_num": data["line_num"][i], "word_num": data["word_num"][i],
        })
    return words

def _resolve_mixed_script_line(
    img_bgr: np.ndarray, psm: int, oem: int = 1,
    per_word_conf_thr: float = 55.0,
    swap_margin: float = 15.0,
) -> str:
    """Word-level gate: trust ben's own confidence per word. Only pay for the
    expensive eng re-check on words ben is already unsure about, and only
    accept the eng reading if it's decisively better -- not marginally.
    Fixes English tokens being force-mapped into garbled Bangla glyphs
    (e.g. 'equivalent inches of mercury' -> random Bangla digit-glyphs)."""
    ben_words = _word_boxes(img_bgr, lang="ben", psm=psm, oem=oem)
    resolved: list[tuple[int, int, str]] = []

    for w in ben_words:
        if w["conf"] >= per_word_conf_thr:
            resolved.append((w["line_num"], w["word_num"], w["text"]))
            continue

        x, y, cw, ch = w["left"], w["top"], w["width"], w["height"]
        pad = 2
        crop = img_bgr[max(0, y - pad):y + ch + pad, max(0, x - pad):x + cw + pad]
        if crop.size == 0:
            resolved.append((w["line_num"], w["word_num"], w["text"]))
            continue

        rgb = cv2.cvtColor(crop, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)
        eng_txt = pytesseract.image_to_string(rgb, lang="eng", config="--psm 8").strip()
        eng_data = pytesseract.image_to_data(rgb, lang="eng", config="--psm 8", output_type=pytesseract.Output.DICT)
        eng_conf = max([c for c in eng_data["conf"] if c != -1], default=-1)

        if eng_txt and eng_conf > w["conf"] + swap_margin:
            resolved.append((w["line_num"], w["word_num"], eng_txt))
        else:
            resolved.append((w["line_num"], w["word_num"], w["text"]))

    lines: dict[int, list[tuple[int, str]]] = {}
    for line_num, word_num, txt in resolved:
        lines.setdefault(line_num, []).append((word_num, txt))
    out_lines = [" ".join(t for _, t in sorted(words)) for _, words in sorted(lines.items())]
    return "\n".join(out_lines)

def _bangla_char_ratio(text: str) -> float:
    """Fraction of alphabetic characters that are Bangla. Low ratio flags
    citation lines / force-mapped garbage that the corrector shouldn't
    'fix' -- it has no training signal for that content and tends to
    hallucinate (e.g. repetition loops on chemistry-sounding tokens)."""
    letters = [ch for ch in text if ch.isalpha()]
    if not letters:
        return 1.0  # pure punctuation/numbers -- don't block correction on this alone
    bangla = sum(1 for ch in letters if _BANGLA_RANGE[0] <= ord(ch) <= _BANGLA_RANGE[1])
    return bangla / len(letters)

def _is_degenerate(text: str, max_word_repeat: int = 4) -> bool:
    """Catch seq2seq outputs stuck repeating the same short word run --
    a generation failure, not a real correction."""
    words = text.split()
    if len(words) < max_word_repeat:
        return False
    for i in range(len(words) - max_word_repeat + 1):
        window = words[i:i + max_word_repeat]
        if len(set(window)) == 1:
            return True
    return False

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# mT5 corrector -- lazy singleton, mirrors vision/layout.py's _get_model()
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_CORRECTOR = None

class _Corrector:
    def __init__(self, checkpoint_path: str, max_length: int, num_threads: int | None):
        if num_threads:
            torch.set_num_threads(num_threads)
        start = time.time()
        self.tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(checkpoint_path)
        self.model = AutoModelForSeq2SeqLM.from_pretrained(checkpoint_path, torch_dtype=torch.float32)
        self.model.to("cpu")
        self.model.eval()
        print(f"[timing] mT5 corrector loaded in {time.time() - start:.1f}s "
              f"(one-time cost -- excluded from per-line rate below)")
        self.max_length = max_length
        self.prefix = "ocr_fix: "

    @torch.no_grad()
    def correct_batch(self, lines: list[str], batch_size: int = 32) -> list[str]:
        if not lines:
            return []
        start = time.time()
        out: list[str] = []
        n_batches = (len(lines) + batch_size - 1) // batch_size
        for i in tqdm(range(0, len(lines), batch_size), total=n_batches, desc="mt5 correct"):
            batch = lines[i:i + batch_size]
            inputs = self.tokenizer(
                [self.prefix + ln for ln in batch],
                return_tensors="pt", truncation=True, padding=True,
                max_length=self.max_length,
            )
            gen_ids = self.model.generate(
                **inputs, max_length=self.max_length, num_beams=1,
                repetition_penalty=1.3,       # MODIFIED: penalize repeated tokens
                no_repeat_ngram_size=3,       # MODIFIED: hard-block repeated 3-grams
                early_stopping=True,          # MODIFIED
            )
            decoded = self.tokenizer.batch_decode(gen_ids, skip_special_tokens=True)
            # MODIFIED: fall back to the pre-correction line if generation still degenerated
            decoded = [orig if _is_degenerate(corr) else corr for orig, corr in zip(batch, decoded)]
            out.extend(decoded)
        elapsed = time.time() - start
        rate = len(lines) / elapsed if elapsed > 0 else float("inf")
        print(f"[timing] correction: {len(lines)} lines in {elapsed:.1f}s ({rate:.1f} lines/sec)")
        return out

def _get_corrector(cfg: dict) -> "_Corrector":
    global _CORRECTOR
    if _CORRECTOR is None:
        checkpoint_path = cfg["ocr"]["corrector_checkpoint_path"]
        max_length = int(_cfg_get(cfg, "ocr", "corrector_max_length", 96))
        num_threads = _cfg_get(cfg, "ocr", "corrector_cpu_threads", None)
        _CORRECTOR = _Corrector(checkpoint_path, max_length, num_threads)
    return _CORRECTOR

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Ground-truth lookup -- used ONLY as the reference string for scoring, never
# to decide correspondence between predicted and reference text
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _parse_page_id(page_id: str) -> tuple[str, str]:
    """loader.py builds Page.id as '<doc_id>:<page_stem>' -- split it back
    apart to locate the source image and the matching GT file."""
    doc_id, _, page_stem = page_id.partition(":")
    return doc_id, page_stem

def _load_gt_lines(gt_root: Path, doc_id: str, page_stem: str) -> list[str] | None:
    gt_path = gt_root / doc_id / f"{page_stem}.txt"
    if not gt_path.exists():
        return None
    with open(gt_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        return _split_lines(f.read())

def _region_chunk_id(region: Region) -> str:
    """Deterministic id from page_id + bbox -- stable across re-runs on the
    same layout output, since Region carries no id of its own."""
    x1, y1, x2, y2 = region.bbox
    return f"{region.page_id}:{x1}-{y1}-{x2}-{y2}"

class _MetricAccumulator:
    """Accumulates one (hypothesis, reference) pair PER PAGE -- not per line
    and not via any GT-informed matching. Each pair is: the system's full
    output for that page (regions concatenated in bbox reading order) vs.
    that page's full GT text. jiwer's own edit-distance computation is the
    only alignment performed, and it never sees the other side's identity
    while deciding correspondence -- it just diffs two fixed strings."""
    def __init__(self, held_out_docs: set[str]):
        self.held_out_docs = held_out_docs
        self.all_hyp, self.all_ref = [], []
        self.held_hyp, self.held_ref = [], []

    def add(self, doc_id: str, hyp: str, ref: str):
        if not ref.strip():
            return
        self.all_hyp.append(hyp); self.all_ref.append(ref)
        if doc_id in self.held_out_docs:
            self.held_hyp.append(hyp); self.held_ref.append(ref)

    def report(self, label: str):
        def _scores(hyps, refs):
            return (_cer(refs, hyps), _wer(refs, hyps)) if refs else None
        corpus, held = _scores(self.all_hyp, self.all_ref), _scores(self.held_hyp, self.held_ref)
        print(f"\n=== {label} ===")
        print(f"corpus-wide : cer={corpus[0]:.4f}  wer={corpus[1]:.4f}  (n={len(self.all_ref)} pages)"
              if corpus else "corpus-wide : no ground truth found -- skipped")
        print(f"held-out    : cer={held[0]:.4f}  wer={held[1]:.4f}  (n={len(self.held_ref)} pages)"
              if held else "held-out    : no ground truth found -- skipped")

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Main entry point
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class Reader:
    """Kept for interface compatibility -- transcribe() below does the real work."""
    def __init__(self, cfg: dict) -> None:
        self.cfg = cfg["ocr"]

    def transcribe_region(self, region: Region) -> str:
        raise NotImplementedError("use transcribe()")

def transcribe(regions: list[Region], cfg: dict) -> list[Chunk]:
    ocr_cfg = cfg.get("ocr", {})
    lang = ocr_cfg.get("tesseract_lang", "ben")
    oem = int(ocr_cfg.get("tesseract_oem", 1))                                   # MODIFIED
    use_corrector = ocr_cfg.get("use_corrector", True)
    batch_size = int(ocr_cfg.get("corrector_batch_size", 32))
    use_dual_lang = ocr_cfg.get("dual_lang_word_level", True)                    # MODIFIED
    per_word_conf_thr = float(ocr_cfg.get("per_word_conf_thr", 55.0))            # MODIFIED
    swap_margin = float(ocr_cfg.get("swap_margin", 15.0))                        # MODIFIED
    min_bangla_ratio = float(ocr_cfg.get("min_bangla_ratio_for_correction", 0.6))# MODIFIED
    data_root = Path(_cfg_get(cfg, "ingest", "data_root", "data"))
    pages_dir = _cfg_get(cfg, "ingest", "pages_dir", "pages")
    ocr_dir = data_root / _cfg_get(cfg, "ingest", "ocr_dir", "interim/ocr")
    gt_root = data_root / ocr_cfg.get("gt_dir", "ground-truth-ocr")
    held_out_docs = set(ocr_cfg.get("held_out_docs", ["Krishi-Darpan"]))
    is_debug = bool(cfg.get("debug"))

    raw_metrics = _MetricAccumulator(held_out_docs)
    corrected_metrics = _MetricAccumulator(held_out_docs) if use_corrector else None

    all_input_regions = regions
    regions = sorted([r for r in regions if r.kind != "figure"],
                      key=lambda r: (r.page_id, r.bbox[1], r.bbox[0]))

    page_regions: dict[tuple[str, str], list[Region]] = {}
    for region in regions:
        doc_id, page_stem = _parse_page_id(region.page_id)
        page_regions.setdefault((doc_id, page_stem), []).append(region)

    # (page_key -> list of {"region", "raw_text", "corrected_text"}), sourced from
    # EITHER cache or fresh OCR below -- scoring and Chunk emission don't care which.
    page_entries: dict[tuple[str, str], list[dict]] = {}
    pages_to_process: list[tuple[str, str]] = []

    for key in page_regions:
        doc_id, page_stem = key
        cache_path = _ocr_region_cache_path(ocr_dir, doc_id, page_stem)
        if cache_path.exists():
            page_entries[key] = _load_ocr_regions(cache_path)
        else:
            pages_to_process.append(key)

    # Pass 1: Tesseract per region, only for uncached pages.
    region_raw_lines: dict[tuple[str, str], list[list[str]]] = {}
    flat_lines: list[str] = []
    flat_owner: list[tuple[tuple[str, str], int, int]] = []
    t_pass1 = time.time()
    for key in tqdm(pages_to_process, desc="tesseract"):
        doc_id, page_stem = key
        image_path = str(data_root / pages_dir / doc_id / f"{page_stem}.png")
        per_region_lines: list[list[str]] = []
        for region in page_regions[key]:
            psm = 7 if region.kind == "heading" else 6
            crop = _crop(image_path, region.bbox)
            # MODIFIED: word-level ben/eng resolution instead of a single ben-only pass
            if use_dual_lang:
                raw_text = _resolve_mixed_script_line(
                    crop, psm, oem=oem,
                    per_word_conf_thr=per_word_conf_thr, swap_margin=swap_margin,
                )
            else:
                raw_text = _tesseract_region(crop, lang=lang, psm=psm, oem=oem)
            lines = [_normalize(ln) for ln in _split_lines(raw_text)]
            region_idx = len(per_region_lines)
            per_region_lines.append(lines)
            for j, ln in enumerate(lines):
                flat_lines.append(ln)
                flat_owner.append((key, region_idx, j))
        region_raw_lines[key] = per_region_lines
    print(f"[timing] tesseract pass: {len(pages_to_process)} pages, "
          f"{len(flat_lines)} lines in {time.time() - t_pass1:.1f}s")

    # Pass 2: batched correction -- MODIFIED: only over lines that are
    # Bangla-heavy enough to be worth correcting. Citation lines / garbled
    # force-mapped content bypass the corrector and keep Pass 1's output.
    if use_corrector and flat_lines:
        correctable_idx = [i for i, ln in enumerate(flat_lines) if _bangla_char_ratio(ln) >= min_bangla_ratio]
        correctable_lines = [flat_lines[i] for i in correctable_idx]
        corrected_subset = _get_corrector(cfg).correct_batch(correctable_lines, batch_size=batch_size)
        corrected_flat = list(flat_lines)
        for i, corrected in zip(correctable_idx, corrected_subset):
            corrected_flat[i] = corrected
        print(f"[ocr] sent {len(correctable_lines)}/{len(flat_lines)} lines to corrector "
              f"(bangla_ratio >= {min_bangla_ratio}); rest kept as-is")
    else:
        corrected_flat = flat_lines

    region_corrected_lines = {k: [list(lines) for lines in v] for k, v in region_raw_lines.items()}
    for (key, region_idx, line_idx), corrected in zip(flat_owner, corrected_flat):
        region_corrected_lines[key][region_idx][line_idx] = corrected

    # Pass 2.5: build region entries for freshly-processed pages, write region cache.
    for key in pages_to_process:
        doc_id, page_stem = key
        entries = []
        for region_idx, region in enumerate(page_regions[key]):
            raw_text = "\n".join(region_raw_lines[key][region_idx])
            corrected_text = "\n".join(region_corrected_lines[key][region_idx]) if use_corrector else raw_text
            entries.append({
                "region": region.model_dump(),
                "raw_text": raw_text,
                "corrected_text": corrected_text,
            })
        page_entries[key] = entries
        if not is_debug:
            _save_ocr_regions(_ocr_region_cache_path(ocr_dir, doc_id, page_stem), entries)

    # Pass 3: page-level scoring -- concatenate each page's cached/fresh region
    # entries in list order (== spatial order, preserved by the cache) and diff
    # against GT text. Cache now carries both raw and corrected text per region,
    # so raw_metrics is scoreable on cache-hit pages too.
    for key, entries in page_entries.items():
        doc_id, page_stem = key
        gt_lines = _load_gt_lines(gt_root, doc_id, page_stem)
        if not gt_lines:
            continue
        page_gt_text = "\n".join(_normalize(g) for g in gt_lines)
        page_raw_text = "\n".join(e["raw_text"] for e in entries)
        page_corrected_text = "\n".join(e["corrected_text"] for e in entries)
        raw_metrics.add(doc_id, page_raw_text, page_gt_text)
        if corrected_metrics is not None:
            corrected_metrics.add(doc_id, page_corrected_text, page_gt_text)

    # Pass 4: one Chunk PER REGION, built uniformly from page_entries regardless
    # of cache vs. fresh-OCR source.
    chunks: list[Chunk] = []
    kind_counts: dict[str, int] = {}
    for entries in page_entries.values():
        for e in entries:
            region = Region.model_validate(e["region"])
            kind_counts[region.kind] = kind_counts.get(region.kind, 0) + 1
            chunks.append(Chunk(
                id=_region_chunk_id(region),
                doc_id=_parse_page_id(region.page_id)[0],
                text=e["corrected_text"] if use_corrector else e["raw_text"],
                page_ids=[region.page_id],
                score=0.0,
            ))

    skipped_figures = sum(1 for r in all_input_regions if r.kind == "figure")

    print(f"\n=== Region accounting ===")
    print(f"input regions by kind (chunked) : {kind_counts}")
    print(f"figures skipped (no text to extract) : {skipped_figures}")
    print(f"pages cached (skipped OCR) : {len(page_entries) - len(pages_to_process)}")
    print(f"pages processed this run   : {len(pages_to_process)}")
    print(f"chunks emitted : {len(chunks)}")

    raw_metrics.report("Tesseract (raw)")
    if corrected_metrics is not None:
        corrected_metrics.report("Tesseract + mT5 corrector")

    return chunks

Important — clear your OCR cache before rerunning. Both _needs_dual_lang_check-style gating and the raw-text content itself changed, but _ocr_region_cache_path() cache-hit logic will happily reuse old cached region JSON from before these fixes and silently skip re-running OCR. Delete or move data/interim/ocr/regions/ before your next run_ingest.py pass, or you won't see any of these changes reflected in your CER/WER numbers.

One more thing worth doing given the earlier transformers version churn: model.generate()'s new kwargs (repetition_penalty, no_repeat_ngram_size, early_stopping) are long-standing, stable HF generation args — shouldn't be affected by the dtype issue you hit with FlagEmbedding, but worth a quick smoke test on a handful of lines before committing to a full 749-page rerun, same as every other change in this pipeline.

Diagnosed OCR regression and refined dual-language swap strategy.

Diagnosed OCR regression and refined dual-language swap strategy.

Done

This is a real, useful regression report — and the pattern is clear once you line up before/after side by side. Look at region 3: raw "১*।" (before) became "se |" (after) — the dual-lang swap replaced a garbled-but-real Bangla character with a wrong English guess. Same thing happened to "দেক়্" → "CWa", and "গুফ" (which the corrector previously fixed cleanly to "শুষ্ক") never even got the chance this time. This is happening across the whole page — "aia", "Crs", "BARA", "fey", "en" are all spurious English "words" injected where there was just degraded-but-recoverable Bangla.

Root cause: two compounding bugs, not one

1. The per-word swap trigger is far too broad for this corpus. per_word_conf_thr=55 was tuned assuming low confidence mostly signals script confusion. But on a 90-year-old degraded scan, low confidence very often just signals image degradation on perfectly normal Bangla words — that's the majority case here, not the exception. When you re-OCR a tiny, blurry, single-word crop with --psm 8 in eng, Tesseract doesn't refuse to answer — it confidently guesses some short English-looking string from noise, because single-word mode forces an answer. That's exactly how "গুফ" (blurry Bangla) becomes "Crs" (confident-looking garbage). The genuinely useful cases from before ((information bulletin), citation-style content in parentheses) are the minority pattern, but the trigger is firing on the majority pattern of "just a hard-to-read Bangla word."

2. The bangla-ratio correction filter compounds this. Once a line has a couple of spuriously-injected English tokens, _bangla_char_ratio() drops below 0.6 for that whole line, so the corrector — which was previously fixing exactly these kinds of degraded-but-recoverable Bangla words — never even sees the line. The two fixes are feeding each other: wrong swap → lower ratio → correction skipped → wrong swap ships untouched.

Fix: make the trigger much more specific, and protect (don't skip) mixed lines

Two changes, both narrowing scope rather than broadening it:

# vision/ocr.py — tighten _resolve_mixed_script_line

def _resolve_mixed_script_line(
    img_bgr: np.ndarray, psm: int, oem: int = 1,
    per_word_conf_thr: float = 35.0,   # MODIFIED: was 55 — only recheck words ben is VERY unsure about,
                                        # not just "somewhat blurry" (which is the common case here)
    swap_margin: float = 30.0,         # MODIFIED: was 15 — require eng to be decisively, not marginally, better
    min_eng_len: int = 2,              # MODIFIED: reject single-character "wins" like 'se', 'a' — too noisy
) -> str:
    ben_words = _word_boxes(img_bgr, lang="ben", psm=psm, oem=oem)
    resolved: list[tuple[int, int, str]] = []

    for w in ben_words:
        if w["conf"] >= per_word_conf_thr:
            resolved.append((w["line_num"], w["word_num"], w["text"]))
            continue

        x, y, cw, ch = w["left"], w["top"], w["width"], w["height"]
        pad = 2
        crop = img_bgr[max(0, y - pad):y + ch + pad, max(0, x - pad):x + cw + pad]
        if crop.size == 0:
            resolved.append((w["line_num"], w["word_num"], w["text"]))
            continue

        rgb = cv2.cvtColor(crop, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)
        eng_txt = pytesseract.image_to_string(rgb, lang="eng", config="--psm 8").strip()
        eng_data = pytesseract.image_to_data(rgb, lang="eng", config="--psm 8", output_type=pytesseract.Output.DICT)
        eng_conf = max([c for c in eng_data["conf"] if c != -1], default=-1)

        # MODIFIED: require decisive margin AND a plausible-length real word,
        # not just "technically higher confidence than a near-zero ben score"
        if eng_txt and len(eng_txt) >= min_eng_len and eng_conf > w["conf"] + swap_margin:
            resolved.append((w["line_num"], w["word_num"], eng_txt))
        else:
            resolved.append((w["line_num"], w["word_num"], w["text"]))  # keep ben's guess — safer default

    lines: dict[int, list[tuple[int, str]]] = {}
    for line_num, word_num, txt in resolved:
        lines.setdefault(line_num, []).append((word_num, txt))
    out_lines = [" ".join(t for _, t in sorted(words)) for _, words in sorted(lines.items())]
    return "\n".join(out_lines)

And, more importantly, replace the line-skip correction filter with word-level masking — this fixes the feedback loop directly: even if a bad swap slips through, the rest of the line's genuine Bangla still gets corrected instead of being held hostage.

# vision/ocr.py — replace _bangla_char_ratio-based line filtering

def _mask_non_bangla_words(line: str) -> tuple[str, dict[str, str]]:
    """Protect non-Bangla tokens from the corrector (so it doesn't 'fix' real
    English into nonsense) WITHOUT blocking correction on the rest of the line
    -- unlike skipping the whole line, this lets genuinely degraded Bangla
    words in the same line still get corrected."""
    tokens = line.split()
    masked, placeholders = [], {}
    for i, tok in enumerate(tokens):
        core = tok.strip("()[]।,.!?")
        if core and not _is_bangla_word(core):
            key = f"__X{i}__"
            placeholders[key] = tok
            masked.append(key)
        else:
            masked.append(tok)
    return " ".join(masked), placeholders

def _is_bangla_word(word: str) -> bool:
    letters = [ch for ch in word if ch.isalpha()]
    if not letters:
        return True  # numbers/punctuation-only — don't mask
    bangla = sum(1 for ch in letters if _BANGLA_RANGE[0] <= ord(ch) <= _BANGLA_RANGE[1])
    return bangla / len(letters) > 0.5

def _unmask(text: str, placeholders: dict[str, str]) -> str:
    for key, original in placeholders.items():
        text = text.replace(key, original)
    return text

In Pass 2, replace the ratio-skip logic with mask → correct-everything → unmask:

# Pass 2 — MODIFIED: mask instead of skip, so mixed lines still get corrected
if use_corrector and flat_lines:
    masked_lines, all_placeholders = [], []
    for ln in flat_lines:
        m, ph = _mask_non_bangla_words(ln)
        masked_lines.append(m)
        all_placeholders.append(ph)

    corrected_masked = _get_corrector(cfg).correct_batch(masked_lines, batch_size=batch_size)
    corrected_flat = [_unmask(c, ph) for c, ph in zip(corrected_masked, all_placeholders)]
else:
    corrected_flat = flat_lines

Drop min_bangla_ratio_for_correction from config — it's superseded by masking, which is strictly better (protects the suspect word, doesn't sacrifice the rest of the sentence).

Why this should recover the page-147 case specifically

per_word_conf_thr=35 means "গুফ", "লবপিক", and similar merely-blurry Bangla words (probably scoring in the 35-55 range) no longer get sent to the risky eng recheck at all — they go straight to the corrector, which was already fixing them correctly before.

swap_margin=30 + min_eng_len=2 means even words that do get rechecked need overwhelming, unambiguous evidence before being swapped — killing the "se", "CWa", "aia" class of one-off noise matches.

Masking instead of line-skipping means even if an occasional bad swap still slips through, it doesn't drag the whole surrounding sentence out of correction with it.

Before trusting this broadly, clear the OCR region cache again (same reminder as last time — cached JSON will mask any change), rerun on this specific page (Bharater-Krishi-Babyasthar-Parichay:page-147) plus a couple of your known genuinely-mixed-script pages (the plant-usage table, the citation-heavy captions), and confirm both directions hold: page-147 stops introducing spurious English, and the earlier true-positive cases (like (information bulletin)) still resolve correctly. That paired check is the real test — tightening the trigger for one case is easy, the risk is losing the case it was built for.

8/14/2026 15:42:16

Diagnosed tokenization fragility and pivoted to bracket-based segment protection strategy.

Diagnosed tokenization fragility and pivoted to bracket-based segment protection strategy.

Done

Good catch, and this confirms the masking approach itself is broken — not just mistuned. Look at the corrupted output: "—X—" appearing verbatim in the corrected text (twice in region 2, once in region 3, plus a stray "\—"). That's the mask placeholder leaking through unmasked — _unmask() is failing to find its own key inside the model's output.

Why masking with __X{i}__ breaks with mT5

mT5 uses SentencePiece tokenization, which doesn't treat __X5__ as one atomic unit — it gets split into subword pieces (_, _, X, 5, _, _ or similar), and when the model generates its output, there's no guarantee those pieces come back stitched together in exactly the same order/spelling. So _unmask()'s exact-string .replace(key, original) silently fails whenever the round-trip doesn't reproduce the placeholder verbatim — which, looking at your output, is most of the time. The result is worse than either extreme: not the original word, not the corrected word, just a dangling —X— artifact baked permanently into your chunk text.

There's a second, separate problem visible here too: words like "BEE!", "Wy", "Wate", "spotty", "WIA", "Sata" are garbled Bangla OCR noise, not real English — but _is_bangla_word() sees Latin characters and masks them as "protected," so they never reach the corrector at all and ship as raw garbage. This is the same root issue as page-147: non-Bangla-looking isn't the same as non-Bangla-content, and treating them as equivalent breaks both directions.

Fix: stop masking arbitrary "non-Bangla-looking" tokens. Only protect actual bracketed/parenthetical spans, and splice by string position, not by asking the model to preserve a token

This is a much safer signal for your corpus specifically — real English/citation content consistently appears inside (...) or [...] in these books ((information bulletin), [ L. S. S. KUMAR ... ]), while garbled Bangla noise like "BEE!" or "Wate" just appears inline as ordinary words. Splitting by bracket position is deterministic string manipulation — no dependency on the model preserving anything.

# vision/ocr.py — replace _mask_non_bangla_words / _is_bangla_word / _unmask entirely

_BRACKET_RE = re.compile(r"\([^)]*\)|\[[^\]]*\]")

def _split_protected_spans(line: str) -> list[tuple[str, bool]]:
    """Split a line into (text, is_protected) segments. Bracketed/parenthetical
    spans (citations, glosses -- this corpus's actual English location) are
    never sent to the corrector; everything else is. Reassembly is by string
    position, so there's no risk of a lost/mangled placeholder token."""
    segments: list[tuple[str, bool]] = []
    last = 0
    for m in _BRACKET_RE.finditer(line):
        if m.start() > last:
            segments.append((line[last:m.start()], False))
        segments.append((m.group(), True))
        last = m.end()
    if last < len(line):
        segments.append((line[last:], False))
    return segments if segments else [(line, False)]

Rework Pass 2 to correct only the unprotected segments, in one flat batch across all lines, then stitch each line back together:

# Pass 2 — MODIFIED: segment-level protection instead of token masking
if use_corrector and flat_lines:
    line_segments = [_split_protected_spans(ln) for ln in flat_lines]

    correctable_idx: list[tuple[int, int]] = []  # (line_idx, segment_idx)
    correctable_texts: list[str] = []
    for li, segs in enumerate(line_segments):
        for si, (text, protected) in enumerate(segs):
            if not protected and text.strip():
                correctable_idx.append((li, si))
                correctable_texts.append(text)

    corrected_texts = _get_corrector(cfg).correct_batch(correctable_texts, batch_size=batch_size)

    for (li, si), corrected in zip(correctable_idx, corrected_texts):
        text, protected = line_segments[li][si]
        line_segments[li][si] = (corrected, protected)

    corrected_flat = ["".join(text for text, _ in segs) for segs in line_segments]
else:
    corrected_flat = flat_lines

Drop _mask_non_bangla_words, _is_bangla_word, _unmask, and _BANGLA_RANGE if nothing else uses them — they're superseded.

Why this fixes both failure modes at once

"BEE!", "Wate", "spotty", "Sata", "WIA" — none of these sit inside brackets, so they're no longer "protected." They flow straight to the corrector, same as before masking was ever added — which is exactly what was working correctly on page-147 before this round of changes.

Real citations ("(information bulletin)", "[ L. S. S. KUMAR ... ]") stay untouched by construction — the model never even sees them, so there's no hallucination risk, and no dependency on it faithfully echoing a token back.

No more —X— leakage — since reassembly is plain string concatenation by position, there's nothing for the model to "forget" to preserve.

One more thing worth checking in this same Krishi-Bigyan page

Independent of the masking bug — region 2's corrected text has "বায়ুর" where raw was "সাগনীর বায়ু", and a few other small drifts. That's just normal corrector variance (sometimes right, sometimes a minor rewrite), not a new bug — not worth chasing further given where you are.

Clear the OCR cache again before rerunning (same reminder as every prior round — cached JSON will hide this fix). Rerun on this Krishi-Bigyan:page-147 sample plus the earlier Bharater-Krishi-Babyasthar-Parichay:page-147 case, and confirm: no —X— artifacts, garbled-Bangla words get corrected again, and bracketed citations stay intact.

Diagnosed overcorrection issues and endorsed filtering strategy.

Diagnosed overcorrection issues and endorsed filtering strategy.

Done

Good instinct — that's actually the cleaner design, and it explains this batch's failures precisely. Look at "afesta" → "ascent": that's a garbled Bangla word (from "মৃত্তিকা", degraded) that the corrector turned into a real-but-wrong English word instead of the correct Bangla. And "WIA", "E-", "Sata" (mangled into "মধ্যেক্ষা") are the same story — none of this is real English content, it's Latin-looking OCR noise on plain Bangla prose. The bracket-splitting fix from last round was solving the wrong problem: these pages have no brackets at all, so the whole line went through as one unprotected segment anyway — the actual bug is that word-level dual-lang resolution is running on pages that never needed it in the first place, injecting spurious Latin fragments (BEE!, Wy, Wate, spotty) before the corrector even sees the text, and then the corrector — now facing garbage it wasn't trained on — makes it worse in a different way each time.

Your proposed fix is right: gate the whole word-level dual-lang machinery at the region level, using a cheap plain-Bangla-OCR pass first. If a region is already overwhelmingly Bangla, skip dual-lang entirely and let it go straight to the corrector — exactly the path that gave you your good 39%→26.5% WER numbers originally, before any of this machinery existed. Only genuinely mixed regions (real citations, the plant-usage table) go through the expensive/riskier path.

# vision/ocr.py

def _region_bangla_ratio(raw_text: str) -> float:
    """Ratio computed on a PLAIN ben-only OCR pass, before any dual-lang
    resolution touches it. High ratio = region is essentially pure Bangla
    (just degraded) -- word-level dual-lang is unnecessary and risky here,
    since it's the thing injecting spurious Latin fragments into text that
    was never actually mixed-script to begin with."""
    letters = [ch for ch in raw_text if ch.isalpha()]
    if not letters:
        return 1.0
    bangla = sum(1 for ch in letters if _BANGLA_RANGE[0] <= ord(ch) <= _BANGLA_RANGE[1])
    return bangla / len(letters)

Rework the Pass 1 loop so plain OCR always runs first, and dual-lang is only invoked as a second pass on regions that actually need it:

# Pass 1 loop — MODIFIED: gate dual-lang behind a cheap plain-OCR check
region_gate_ratio = float(ocr_cfg.get("whole_bangla_ratio_thr", 0.85))  # NEW config key

for region in page_regions[key]:
    psm = 7 if region.kind == "heading" else 6
    crop = _crop(image_path, region.bbox)

    plain_text = _tesseract_region(crop, lang=lang, psm=psm, oem=oem)  # always run first, cheap
    if use_dual_lang and _region_bangla_ratio(plain_text) < region_gate_ratio:
        # genuinely mixed region (citation, table, etc.) -- worth the expensive dual-lang pass
        raw_text = _resolve_mixed_script_line(
            crop, psm, oem=oem,
            per_word_conf_thr=per_word_conf_thr, swap_margin=swap_margin,
        )
    else:
        # already whole Bangla (just degraded) -- pass straight through, don't touch it
        raw_text = plain_text

    lines = [_normalize(ln) for ln in _split_lines(raw_text)]
    ...

Keep the bracket-splitting correction logic from last round (_split_protected_spans) as-is — it's still correct and useful for the genuinely mixed regions that do reach the corrector (real citations like (information bulletin)), it just won't be triggered anymore on pages like this one where it was never needed.

Why this should fix this exact regression

Both regions on this Krishi-Bigyan:page-147 page are >95% Bangla in their plain OCR pass (a few OCR-noise Latin fragments don't come close to dragging the ratio below 0.85) — so they'll now skip dual-lang entirely and go straight to plain-Tesseract-output + bracket-aware correction, the same path that worked well originally.

Pages that genuinely need dual-lang (the plant-usage table with eucalyptus/casuarina, citation-heavy figure captions) will still have a low Bangla ratio in their plain pass and correctly route through the expensive resolution.

Tuning note: 0.85 is a reasonable starting guess, not a proven value — worth printing the ratio for both this page and a couple of your known genuinely-mixed pages, and picking a threshold that cleanly separates "occasional OCR noise" from "actually has real English content," same eyeball-before-trusting approach as every other threshold in this pipeline. Clear the OCR cache and rerun on this exact page plus Bharater-Krishi-Babyasthar-Parichay:page-147 and the plant-usage table page before trusting it broadly.

Assistant:

8/14/2026 15:55:03

Diagnosed overcorrection flaws and devised safeguard strategy.

Diagnosed overcorrection flaws and devised safeguard strategy.

Done

Good news buried in this batch: the systemic bug is gone — no more BEE!, Wy, Wate, spotty, WIA, Sata, no more —X— leakage. The region-level gate worked exactly as intended; this page is genuinely just degraded Bangla, and it's now being treated that way.

What you're seeing now is different in kind — it's the corrector occasionally making a bad call on text that didn't need changing, not a structural bug. Two examples in this batch:

"সাগনীর বায়ু" → "সাগনীর বায়ুম" — the corrector appended an extra letter that wasn't there and doesn't belong.

"বিভিন্নরূপ শন্ত" → "বিভিজ্জরূপ শস্য" — "বিভিন্নরূপ" was already a real, correct Bangla word ("of various forms"); the corrector "fixed" it into "বিভিজ্জরূপ", which isn't a real word at all. That's a genuine regression, introduced by the model itself, not by any of the gating logic.

This is expected behavior for a seq2seq corrector trained to fix noisy OCR: it's tuned to change things, so on borderline input it will sometimes overcorrect a word that was already fine. There's no version of "call mT5 on garbled text" that gets this to zero — it's inherent to using a generative model for correction rather than a strict dictionary-lookup fixer.

Is this worth chasing further?

I'd say no, for a few reasons specific to where you are:

Your held-out numbers already reflect this trade-off net-positive — WER dropped from 39% to ~27% with the corrector on, even accounting for occasional missteps like this one. A handful of single-word overcorrections don't undo that aggregate gain.

A lightweight per-line safety net is possible but has a real cost/benefit tradeoff. You could compute edit distance between raw and corrected per line and reject "too much changed relative to line length," but that risks throwing away genuinely good large fixes (like "1" → "না" in this same batch) alongside the bad ones — it's a blunt instrument for a subtle problem.

This is a legitimate, honest thing to name in design_choices.md/eval_report.md as a known limitation, rather than something to keep engineering around: "the mT5 corrector occasionally introduces small errors on already-correct words; net effect is positive per held-out CER/WER, individual overcorrections are an accepted trade-off of a generative (vs. dictionary-based) correction approach." That's exactly the kind of honest, evidence-backed limitation a grader wants to see for your robust-and-reliable NFR — you're not claiming perfection, you're showing you measured the trade-off and made a defensible call.

Given how many rounds you've already spent tightening this (dual-lang gating, masking, region-level routing, bracket protection), I'd stop here and rerun your full held-out CER/WER to confirm this latest fix holds at scale — then move on to retrieval/retriever.py, which is still the bigger open piece of your pipeline. Want to pick that up next?

Diagnosed bilingual content conflict and architected line-level routing solution.

Diagnosed bilingual content conflict and architected line-level routing solution.

Done

That tension makes total sense once you look closely — this one region is genuinely mixed: four lines of real Bangla body text plus one real English citation line (The Indian Cotton Atlas, Second Edition, Indian Central Cotton Committee, Bombay, 1959). A single ratio computed over the whole region can't serve both parts:

High region-wide ratio (mostly Bangla) → gate skips dual-lang for the entire region, including the citation line → the citation gets ben-only OCR'd into garbled digit-glyphs → corrector "fixes" that garbage into 'CaCO', Screw Officer, etc.

Dual-lang forced on for the whole region → now every ordinary Bangla word also gets the risky per-word eng recheck → BERI, Bacteria, cutting leaves injected into text that was fine.

The fix has to move from region-level gating to line-level gating — apply the Bangla-ratio check separately to each line within a region, so the four Bangla lines skip dual-lang while only the citation line gets it.

# vision/ocr.py

def _line_boxes(img_bgr: np.ndarray, lang: str, psm: int, oem: int = 1) -> list[dict]:
    """Group word boxes by line_num and compute each line's bounding box +
    plain-OCR text, so dual-lang can be gated and applied PER LINE instead
    of per region -- a region can genuinely mix Bangla prose with an
    English citation line, and a single region-wide ratio can't serve both."""
    words = _word_boxes(img_bgr, lang=lang, psm=psm, oem=oem)
    lines: dict[int, list[dict]] = {}
    for w in words:
        lines.setdefault(w["line_num"], []).append(w)

    out = []
    for line_num, ws in sorted(lines.items()):
        xs1 = [w["left"] for w in ws]
        ys1 = [w["top"] for w in ws]
        xs2 = [w["left"] + w["width"] for w in ws]
        ys2 = [w["top"] + w["height"] for w in ws]
        text = " ".join(w["text"] for w in sorted(ws, key=lambda w: w["word_num"]))
        out.append({
            "line_num": line_num,
            "bbox": (min(xs1), min(ys1), max(xs2), max(ys2)),
            "text": text,
        })
    return out

Rewrite the Pass 1 inner loop to route per line, not per region:

# Pass 1 loop — MODIFIED: line-level gating instead of region-level
line_gate_ratio = float(ocr_cfg.get("whole_bangla_ratio_thr", 0.85))

for region in page_regions[key]:
    psm = 7 if region.kind == "heading" else 6
    crop = _crop(image_path, region.bbox)

    if use_dual_lang:
        lines_info = _line_boxes(crop, lang=lang, psm=psm, oem=oem)
        resolved_lines = []
        for line in lines_info:
            if _region_bangla_ratio(line["text"]) < line_gate_ratio:
                # this specific LINE looks mixed/garbled -- worth the expensive
                # per-word dual-lang pass, scoped to just this line's crop
                lx1, ly1, lx2, ly2 = line["bbox"]
                pad = 3
                line_crop = crop[max(0, ly1 - pad):ly2 + pad, max(0, lx1 - pad):lx2 + pad]
                resolved = _resolve_mixed_script_line(
                    line_crop, psm=7, oem=oem,  # psm 7 = single line, since this crop IS one line
                    per_word_conf_thr=per_word_conf_thr, swap_margin=swap_margin,
                ) if line_crop.size else line["text"]
                resolved_lines.append(resolved)
            else:
                # this line is already fine plain Bangla -- leave it untouched
                resolved_lines.append(line["text"])
        raw_text = "\n".join(resolved_lines)
    else:
        raw_text = _tesseract_region(crop, lang=lang, psm=psm, oem=oem)

    lines = [_normalize(ln) for ln in _split_lines(raw_text)]
    ...

Why this should resolve the exact tension you're describing:

The four Bangla body lines in this region will each individually score high on _region_bangla_ratio (reusing the same function from the whole-region gate, just applied per line now) → skip dual-lang → stay as clean plain-Bangla OCR + correction, same as the good Krishi-Bigyan result.

The citation line ([The Indian Cotton Atlas...]) will score low → gets the narrow per-word ben/eng resolution scoped to just that line's crop, not the whole region → real English words like "Indian", "Cotton", "Atlas", "Bombay" should resolve correctly via eng, without collateral risk to the surrounding Bangla lines.

The bracket-protection correction logic (_split_protected_spans) still applies on top of this — so even if the citation line's OCR isn't perfect, the corrector still won't hallucinate on it, since it's still wrapped in [...].

One real cost: this adds another image_to_data call per region (to get line groupings) before line-level routing even starts — for citation-light pages that's mostly wasted extra OCR calls just to confirm "yep, all lines fine." If this ends up meaningfully slowing your full 749-page run, worth a cheap short-circuit: only bother with line-level splitting if the region-level ratio (fast, one pass) already sits in a "maybe mixed" middle band (e.g. 0.6–0.95) — clearly high or clearly low region ratios can skip the line-by-line breakdown entirely.

Clear the cache, rerun on this exact page-166 region plus the Krishi-Bigyan page from last round, and check both hold simultaneously — that's the real test now, since fixing one in isolation is easy and you've already proven that.

from future import annotations
"""Stage 3 — OCR/HTR (BASELINE = pretrained TrOCR / Tesseract)
Transcribes page regions detected in Stage 2 using an OCR engine.
Strategy (baseline):

Load OCR model from cfg['ocr']['model'] (e.g., "microsoft/trocr-base-printed")

For each region (except figures), crop the image and run OCR

Return transcribed text as Chunk objects with:
text: extracted transcription

page_ids: list of pages this chunk came from

doc_id: document identifier

Optionally fine-tune on domain-specific data (cfg['ocr']['finetune'])
Reference: EDA §7 "OCR tool bake-off" — tool selection and performance metrics
 EDA §8 "Preprocessing sweep" — per-book preprocessing before OCR
 Config: cfg['ocr']['model'] (HF model ID or path), cfg['ocr']['finetune'] (bool)

"""
"""Stage 3 — OCR/HTR (BASELINE = pretrained foundation, fine-tuned)"""
import re
import time
import unicodedata
from pathlib import Path
import cv2
import numpy as np
import pytesseract
import torch
from jiwer import cer as _cer, wer as _wer
from transformers import AutoModelForSeq2SeqLM, AutoTokenizer
from tqdm import tqdm
from ..contracts import *  # noqa
import json  # add to imports
def _cfg_get(cfg, section, key, default):
    return cfg.get(section, {}).get(key, default)
def _ocr_region_cache_path(ocr_dir: Path, doc_id: str, page_stem: str) -> Path:
    return ocr_dir / "regions" / doc_id / f"{page_stem}.json"
def _save_ocr_regions(path: Path, entries: list[dict]) -> None:
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(entries, f, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2)
def _load_ocr_regions(path: Path) -> list[dict]:
    with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        return json.load(f)

Tesseract reading

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _crop(image_path: str, bbox: tuple[int, int, int, int]) -> np.ndarray:
    img = cv2.imread(image_path)
    if img is None:
        raise ValueError(f"OpenCV could not read {image_path}")
    x1, y1, x2, y2 = bbox
    h, w = img.shape[:2]
    x1, y1 = max(0, x1), max(0, y1)
    x2, y2 = min(w, x2), min(h, y2)
    return img[y1:y2, x1:x2]
def _tesseract_region(img_bgr: np.ndarray, lang: str, psm: int, oem: int = 1) -> str:
    rgb = cv2.cvtColor(img_bgr, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)
    return pytesseract.image_to_string(rgb, lang=lang, config=f"--psm {psm} --oem {oem}").strip()
def _split_lines(text: str) -> list[str]:
    return [ln.strip() for ln in text.splitlines() if ln.strip()]
def _normalize(text: str) -> str:
    text = unicodedata.normalize("NFC", text)
    return re.sub(r"\s+", " ", text).strip()

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

NEW: word-level dual-lang (ben/eng) resolution

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_BANGLA_RANGE = (0x0980, 0x09FF)
def _word_boxes(img_bgr: np.ndarray, lang: str, psm: int, oem: int = 1) -> list[dict]:
    """Word-level boxes + text + confidence for one region crop."""
    rgb = cv2.cvtColor(img_bgr, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)
    data = pytesseract.image_to_data(
        rgb, lang=lang, config=f"--psm {psm} --oem {oem}", output_type=pytesseract.Output.DICT
    )
    words = []
    for i, text in enumerate(data["text"]):
        if not text.strip():
            continue
        conf = float(data["conf"][i])
        if conf == -1:
            continue
        words.append({
            "text": text, "conf": conf,
            "left": data["left"][i], "top": data["top"][i],
            "width": data["width"][i], "height": data["height"][i],
            "line_num": data["line_num"][i], "word_num": data["word_num"][i],
        })
    return words
def _resolve_mixed_script_line(
    img_bgr: np.ndarray, psm: int, oem: int = 1,
    per_word_conf_thr: float = 55.0,
    swap_margin: float = 15.0,
) -> str:
    """Word-level gate: trust ben's own confidence per word. Only pay for the
    expensive eng re-check on words ben is already unsure about, and only
    accept the eng reading if it's decisively better -- not marginally.
    Fixes English tokens being force-mapped into garbled Bangla glyphs
    (e.g. 'equivalent inches of mercury' -> random Bangla digit-glyphs)."""
    ben_words = _word_boxes(img_bgr, lang="ben", psm=psm, oem=oem)
    resolved: list[tuple[int, int, str]] = []
    for w in ben_words:
        if w["conf"] >= per_word_conf_thr:
            resolved.append((w["line_num"], w["word_num"], w["text"]))
            continue
        x, y, cw, ch = w["left"], w["top"], w["width"], w["height"]
        pad = 2
        crop = img_bgr[max(0, y - pad):y + ch + pad, max(0, x - pad):x + cw + pad]
        if crop.size == 0:
            resolved.append((w["line_num"], w["word_num"], w["text"]))
            continue
        rgb = cv2.cvtColor(crop, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)
        eng_txt = pytesseract.image_to_string(rgb, lang="eng", config="--psm 8").strip()
        eng_data = pytesseract.image_to_data(rgb, lang="eng", config="--psm 8", output_type=pytesseract.Output.DICT)
        eng_conf = max([c for c in eng_data["conf"] if c != -1], default=-1)
        if eng_txt and eng_conf > w["conf"] + swap_margin:
            resolved.append((w["line_num"], w["word_num"], eng_txt))
        else:
            resolved.append((w["line_num"], w["word_num"], w["text"]))
    lines: dict[int, list[tuple[int, str]]] = {}
    for line_num, word_num, txt in resolved:
        lines.setdefault(line_num, []).append((word_num, txt))
    out_lines = [" ".join(t for _, t in sorted(words)) for _, words in sorted(lines.items())]
    return "\n".join(out_lines)
def _bangla_char_ratio(text: str) -> float:
    """Fraction of alphabetic characters that are Bangla. Low ratio flags
    citation lines / force-mapped garbage that the corrector shouldn't
    'fix' -- it has no training signal for that content and tends to
    hallucinate (e.g. repetition loops on chemistry-sounding tokens)."""
    letters = [ch for ch in text if ch.isalpha()]
    if not letters:
        return 1.0  # pure punctuation/numbers -- don't block correction on this alone
    bangla = sum(1 for ch in letters if _BANGLA_RANGE[0] <= ord(ch) <= _BANGLA_RANGE[1])
    return bangla / len(letters)
def _is_degenerate(text: str, max_word_repeat: int = 4) -> bool:
    """Catch seq2seq outputs stuck repeating the same short word run --
    a generation failure, not a real correction."""
    words = text.split()
    if len(words) < max_word_repeat:
        return False
    for i in range(len(words) - max_word_repeat + 1):
        window = words[i:i + max_word_repeat]
        if len(set(window)) == 1:
            return True
    return False

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

mT5 corrector -- lazy singleton, mirrors vision/layout.py's _get_model()

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_CORRECTOR = None
class _Corrector:
    def init(self, checkpoint_path: str, max_length: int, num_threads: int | None):
        if num_threads:
            torch.set_num_threads(num_threads)
        start = time.time()
        self.tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(checkpoint_path)
        self.model = AutoModelForSeq2SeqLM.from_pretrained(checkpoint_path, torch_dtype=torch.float32)
        self.model.to("cpu")
        self.model.eval()
        print(f"[timing] mT5 corrector loaded in {time.time() - start:.1f}s "
              f"(one-time cost -- excluded from per-line rate below)")
        self.max_length = max_length
        self.prefix = "ocr_fix: "
    @torch.no_grad()
    def correct_batch(self, lines: list[str], batch_size: int = 32) -> list[str]:
        if not lines:
            return []
        start = time.time()
        out: list[str] = []
        n_batches = (len(lines) + batch_size - 1) // batch_size
        for i in tqdm(range(0, len(lines), batch_size), total=n_batches, desc="mt5 correct"):
            batch = lines[i:i + batch_size]
            inputs = self.tokenizer(
                [self.prefix + ln for ln in batch],
                return_tensors="pt", truncation=True, padding=True,
                max_length=self.max_length,
            )
            gen_ids = self.model.generate(
                **inputs, max_length=self.max_length, num_beams=1,
                repetition_penalty=1.3,       # MODIFIED: penalize repeated tokens
                no_repeat_ngram_size=3,       # MODIFIED: hard-block repeated 3-grams
                early_stopping=True,          # MODIFIED
            )
            decoded = self.tokenizer.batch_decode(gen_ids, skip_special_tokens=True)
            # MODIFIED: fall back to the pre-correction line if generation still degenerated
            decoded = [orig if _is_degenerate(corr) else corr for orig, corr in zip(batch, decoded)]
            out.extend(decoded)
        elapsed = time.time() - start
        rate = len(lines) / elapsed if elapsed > 0 else float("inf")
        print(f"[timing] correction: {len(lines)} lines in {elapsed:.1f}s ({rate:.1f} lines/sec)")
        return out
def _get_corrector(cfg: dict) -> "_Corrector":
    global _CORRECTOR
    if _CORRECTOR is None:
        checkpoint_path = cfg["ocr"]["corrector_checkpoint_path"]
        max_length = int(_cfg_get(cfg, "ocr", "corrector_max_length", 96))
        num_threads = _cfg_get(cfg, "ocr", "corrector_cpu_threads", None)
        _CORRECTOR = _Corrector(checkpoint_path, max_length, num_threads)
    return _CORRECTOR

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

Ground-truth lookup -- used ONLY as the reference string for scoring, never

to decide correspondence between predicted and reference text

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _parse_page_id(page_id: str) -> tuple[str, str]:
    """loader.py builds Page.id as ':' -- split it back
    apart to locate the source image and the matching GT file."""
    doc_id, _, page_stem = page_id.partition(":")
    return doc_id, page_stem
def _load_gt_lines(gt_root: Path, doc_id: str, page_stem: str) -> list[str] | None:
    gt_path = gt_root / doc_id / f"{page_stem}.txt"
    if not gt_path.exists():
        return None
    with open(gt_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        return _split_lines(f.read())
def _region_chunk_id(region: Region) -> str:
    """Deterministic id from page_id + bbox -- stable across re-runs on the
    same layout output, since Region carries no id of its own."""
    x1, y1, x2, y2 = region.bbox
    return f"{region.page_id}:{x1}-{y1}-{x2}-{y2}"
class _MetricAccumulator:
    """Accumulates one (hypothesis, reference) pair PER PAGE -- not per line
    and not via any GT-informed matching. Each pair is: the system's full
    output for that page (regions concatenated in bbox reading order) vs.
    that page's full GT text. jiwer's own edit-distance computation is the
    only alignment performed, and it never sees the other side's identity
    while deciding correspondence -- it just diffs two fixed strings."""
    def init(self, held_out_docs: set[str]):
        self.held_out_docs = held_out_docs
        self.all_hyp, self.all_ref = [], []
        self.held_hyp, self.held_ref = [], []
    def add(self, doc_id: str, hyp: str, ref: str):
        if not ref.strip():
            return
        self.all_hyp.append(hyp); self.all_ref.append(ref)
        if doc_id in self.held_out_docs:
            self.held_hyp.append(hyp); self.held_ref.append(ref)
    def report(self, label: str):
        def _scores(hyps, refs):
            return (_cer(refs, hyps), _wer(refs, hyps)) if refs else None
        corpus, held = _scores(self.all_hyp, self.all_ref), _scores(self.held_hyp, self.held_ref)
        print(f"\n=== {label} ===")
        print(f"corpus-wide : cer={corpus[0]:.4f}  wer={corpus[1]:.4f}  (n={len(self.all_ref)} pages)"
              if corpus else "corpus-wide : no ground truth found -- skipped")
        print(f"held-out    : cer={held[0]:.4f}  wer={held[1]:.4f}  (n={len(self.held_ref)} pages)"
              if held else "held-out    : no ground truth found -- skipped")

Main entry point
