class Reader:
    """Kept for interface compatibility -- transcribe() below does the real work."""
    def init(self, cfg: dict) -> None:
        self.cfg = cfg["ocr"]
    def transcribe_region(self, region: Region) -> str:
        raise NotImplementedError("use transcribe()")
def transcribe(regions: list[Region], cfg: dict) -> list[Chunk]:
    ocr_cfg = cfg.get("ocr", {})
    lang = ocr_cfg.get("tesseract_lang", "ben")
    oem = int(ocr_cfg.get("tesseract_oem", 1))                                   # MODIFIED
    use_corrector = ocr_cfg.get("use_corrector", True)
    batch_size = int(ocr_cfg.get("corrector_batch_size", 32))
    use_dual_lang = ocr_cfg.get("dual_lang_word_level", True)                    # MODIFIED
    per_word_conf_thr = float(ocr_cfg.get("per_word_conf_thr", 55.0))            # MODIFIED
    swap_margin = float(ocr_cfg.get("swap_margin", 15.0))                        # MODIFIED
    min_bangla_ratio = float(ocr_cfg.get("min_bangla_ratio_for_correction", 0.6))# MODIFIED
    data_root = Path(_cfg_get(cfg, "ingest", "data_root", "data"))
    pages_dir = _cfg_get(cfg, "ingest", "pages_dir", "pages")
    ocr_dir = data_root / _cfg_get(cfg, "ingest", "ocr_dir", "interim/ocr")
    gt_root = data_root / ocr_cfg.get("gt_dir", "ground-truth-ocr")
    held_out_docs = set(ocr_cfg.get("held_out_docs", ["Krishi-Darpan"]))
    is_debug = bool(cfg.get("debug"))
    raw_metrics = _MetricAccumulator(held_out_docs)
    corrected_metrics = _MetricAccumulator(held_out_docs) if use_corrector else None
    all_input_regions = regions
    regions = sorted([r for r in regions if r.kind != "figure"],
                      key=lambda r: (r.page_id, r.bbox[1], r.bbox[0]))
    page_regions: dict[tuple[str, str], list[Region]] = {}
    for region in regions:
        doc_id, page_stem = _parse_page_id(region.page_id)
        page_regions.setdefault((doc_id, page_stem), []).append(region)
    # (page_key -> list of {"region", "raw_text", "corrected_text"}), sourced from
    # EITHER cache or fresh OCR below -- scoring and Chunk emission don't care which.
    page_entries: dict[tuple[str, str], list[dict]] = {}
    pages_to_process: list[tuple[str, str]] = []
    for key in page_regions:
        doc_id, page_stem = key
        cache_path = _ocr_region_cache_path(ocr_dir, doc_id, page_stem)
        if cache_path.exists():
            page_entries[key] = _load_ocr_regions(cache_path)
        else:
            pages_to_process.append(key)
    # Pass 1: Tesseract per region, only for uncached pages.
    region_raw_lines: dict[tuple[str, str], list[list[str]]] = {}
    flat_lines: list[str] = []
    flat_owner: list[tuple[tuple[str, str], int, int]] = []
    t_pass1 = time.time()
    for key in tqdm(pages_to_process, desc="tesseract"):
        doc_id, page_stem = key
        image_path = str(data_root / pages_dir / doc_id / f"{page_stem}.png")
        per_region_lines: list[list[str]] = []
        for region in page_regions[key]:
            psm = 7 if region.kind == "heading" else 6
            crop = _crop(image_path, region.bbox)
            # MODIFIED: word-level ben/eng resolution instead of a single ben-only pass
            if use_dual_lang:
                raw_text = _resolve_mixed_script_line(
                    crop, psm, oem=oem,
                    per_word_conf_thr=per_word_conf_thr, swap_margin=swap_margin,
                )
            else:
                raw_text = _tesseract_region(crop, lang=lang, psm=psm, oem=oem)
            lines = [_normalize(ln) for ln in _split_lines(raw_text)]
            region_idx = len(per_region_lines)
            per_region_lines.append(lines)
            for j, ln in enumerate(lines):
                flat_lines.append(ln)
                flat_owner.append((key, region_idx, j))
        region_raw_lines[key] = per_region_lines
    print(f"[timing] tesseract pass: {len(pages_to_process)} pages, "
          f"{len(flat_lines)} lines in {time.time() - t_pass1:.1f}s")
    # Pass 2: batched correction -- MODIFIED: only over lines that are
    # Bangla-heavy enough to be worth correcting. Citation lines / garbled
    # force-mapped content bypass the corrector and keep Pass 1's output.
    if use_corrector and flat_lines:
        correctable_idx = [i for i, ln in enumerate(flat_lines) if _bangla_char_ratio(ln) >= min_bangla_ratio]
        correctable_lines = [flat_lines[i] for i in correctable_idx]
        corrected_subset = _get_corrector(cfg).correct_batch(correctable_lines, batch_size=batch_size)
        corrected_flat = list(flat_lines)
        for i, corrected in zip(correctable_idx, corrected_subset):
            corrected_flat[i] = corrected
        print(f"[ocr] sent {len(correctable_lines)}/{len(flat_lines)} lines to corrector "
              f"(bangla_ratio >= {min_bangla_ratio}); rest kept as-is")
    else:
        corrected_flat = flat_lines
    region_corrected_lines = {k: [list(lines) for lines in v] for k, v in region_raw_lines.items()}
    for (key, region_idx, line_idx), corrected in zip(flat_owner, corrected_flat):
        region_corrected_lines[key][region_idx][line_idx] = corrected
    # Pass 2.5: build region entries for freshly-processed pages, write region cache.
    for key in pages_to_process:
        doc_id, page_stem = key
        entries = []
        for region_idx, region in enumerate(page_regions[key]):
            raw_text = "\n".join(region_raw_lines[key][region_idx])
            corrected_text = "\n".join(region_corrected_lines[key][region_idx]) if use_corrector else raw_text
            entries.append({
                "region": region.model_dump(),
                "raw_text": raw_text,
                "corrected_text": corrected_text,
            })
        page_entries[key] = entries
        if not is_debug:
            _save_ocr_regions(_ocr_region_cache_path(ocr_dir, doc_id, page_stem), entries)
    # Pass 3: page-level scoring -- concatenate each page's cached/fresh region
    # entries in list order (== spatial order, preserved by the cache) and diff
    # against GT text. Cache now carries both raw and corrected text per region,
    # so raw_metrics is scoreable on cache-hit pages too.
    for key, entries in page_entries.items():
        doc_id, page_stem = key
        gt_lines = _load_gt_lines(gt_root, doc_id, page_stem)
        if not gt_lines:
            continue
        page_gt_text = "\n".join(_normalize(g) for g in gt_lines)
        page_raw_text = "\n".join(e["raw_text"] for e in entries)
        page_corrected_text = "\n".join(e["corrected_text"] for e in entries)
        raw_metrics.add(doc_id, page_raw_text, page_gt_text)
        if corrected_metrics is not None:
            corrected_metrics.add(doc_id, page_corrected_text, page_gt_text)
    # Pass 4: one Chunk PER REGION, built uniformly from page_entries regardless
    # of cache vs. fresh-OCR source.
    chunks: list[Chunk] = []
    kind_counts: dict[str, int] = {}
    for entries in page_entries.values():
        for e in entries:
            region = Region.model_validate(e["region"])
            kind_counts[region.kind] = kind_counts.get(region.kind, 0) + 1
            chunks.append(Chunk(
                id=_region_chunk_id(region),
                doc_id=_parse_page_id(region.page_id)[0],
                text=e["corrected_text"] if use_corrector else e["raw_text"],
                page_ids=[region.page_id],
                score=0.0,
            ))
    skipped_figures = sum(1 for r in all_input_regions if r.kind == "figure")
    print(f"\n=== Region accounting ===")
    print(f"input regions by kind (chunked) : {kind_counts}")
    print(f"figures skipped (no text to extract) : {skipped_figures}")
    print(f"pages cached (skipped OCR) : {len(page_entries) - len(pages_to_process)}")
    print(f"pages processed this run   : {len(pages_to_process)}")
    print(f"chunks emitted : {len(chunks)}")
    raw_metrics.report("Tesseract (raw)")
    if corrected_metrics is not None:
        corrected_metrics.report("Tesseract + mT5 corrector")
    return chunks
from future import annotations
"""Stage 3 — OCR/HTR (BASELINE = pretrained TrOCR / Tesseract)

Transcribes page regions detected in Stage 2 using an OCR engine.

Strategy (baseline):

Load OCR model from cfg['ocr']['model'] (e.g., "microsoft/trocr-base-printed")

For each region (except figures), crop the image and run OCR

Return transcribed text as Chunk objects with:
text: extracted transcription

page_ids: list of pages this chunk came from

doc_id: document identifier

Optionally fine-tune on domain-specific data (cfg['ocr']['finetune'])

Reference: EDA §7 "OCR tool bake-off" — tool selection and performance metrics
          EDA §8 "Preprocessing sweep" — per-book preprocessing before OCR
          Config: cfg['ocr']['model'] (HF model ID or path), cfg['ocr']['finetune'] (bool)
"""
"""Stage 3 — OCR/HTR (BASELINE = pretrained foundation, fine-tuned)"""

import re
import time
import unicodedata
from pathlib import Path

import cv2
import numpy as np
import pytesseract
import torch
from jiwer import cer as _cer, wer as _wer
from transformers import AutoModelForSeq2SeqLM, AutoTokenizer
from tqdm import tqdm

from ..contracts import *  # noqa
import json  # add to imports

def _cfg_get(cfg, section, key, default):
    return cfg.get(section, {}).get(key, default)

def _ocr_region_cache_path(ocr_dir: Path, doc_id: str, page_stem: str) -> Path:
    return ocr_dir / "regions" / doc_id / f"{page_stem}.json"

def _save_ocr_regions(path: Path, entries: list[dict]) -> None:
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(entries, f, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2)

def _load_ocr_regions(path: Path) -> list[dict]:
    with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        return json.load(f)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

Tesseract reading

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _crop(image_path: str, bbox: tuple[int, int, int, int]) -> np.ndarray:
    img = cv2.imread(image_path)
    if img is None:
        raise ValueError(f"OpenCV could not read {image_path}")
    x1, y1, x2, y2 = bbox
    h, w = img.shape[:2]
    x1, y1 = max(0, x1), max(0, y1)
    x2, y2 = min(w, x2), min(h, y2)
    return img[y1:y2, x1:x2]

def _tesseract_region(img_bgr: np.ndarray, lang: str, psm: int, oem: int = 1) -> str:
    rgb = cv2.cvtColor(img_bgr, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)
    return pytesseract.image_to_string(rgb, lang=lang, config=f"--psm {psm} --oem {oem}").strip()

def _split_lines(text: str) -> list[str]:
    return [ln.strip() for ln in text.splitlines() if ln.strip()]

def _normalize(text: str) -> str:
    text = unicodedata.normalize("NFC", text)
    return re.sub(r"\s+", " ", text).strip()

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

NEW: word-level dual-lang (ben/eng) resolution

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_BANGLA_RANGE = (0x0980, 0x09FF)

def _word_boxes(img_bgr: np.ndarray, lang: str, psm: int, oem: int = 1) -> list[dict]:
    """Word-level boxes + text + confidence for one region crop."""
    rgb = cv2.cvtColor(img_bgr, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)
    data = pytesseract.image_to_data(
        rgb, lang=lang, config=f"--psm {psm} --oem {oem}", output_type=pytesseract.Output.DICT
    )
    words = []
    for i, text in enumerate(data["text"]):
        if not text.strip():
            continue
        conf = float(data["conf"][i])
        if conf == -1:
            continue
        words.append({
            "text": text, "conf": conf,
            "left": data["left"][i], "top": data["top"][i],
            "width": data["width"][i], "height": data["height"][i],
            "line_num": data["line_num"][i], "word_num": data["word_num"][i],
        })
    return words

def _resolve_mixed_script_line(
    img_bgr: np.ndarray, psm: int, oem: int = 1,
    per_word_conf_thr: float = 55.0,
    swap_margin: float = 15.0,
) -> str:
    """Word-level gate: trust ben's own confidence per word. Only pay for the
    expensive eng re-check on words ben is already unsure about, and only
    accept the eng reading if it's decisively better -- not marginally.
    Fixes English tokens being force-mapped into garbled Bangla glyphs
    (e.g. 'equivalent inches of mercury' -> random Bangla digit-glyphs)."""
    ben_words = _word_boxes(img_bgr, lang="ben", psm=psm, oem=oem)
    resolved: list[tuple[int, int, str]] = []

for w in ben_words:
    if w["conf"] >= per_word_conf_thr:
        resolved.append((w["line_num"], w["word_num"], w["text"]))
        continue

    x, y, cw, ch = w["left"], w["top"], w["width"], w["height"]
    pad = 2
    crop = img_bgr[max(0, y - pad):y + ch + pad, max(0, x - pad):x + cw + pad]
    if crop.size == 0:
        resolved.append((w["line_num"], w["word_num"], w["text"]))
        continue

    rgb = cv2.cvtColor(crop, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)
    eng_txt = pytesseract.image_to_string(rgb, lang="eng", config="--psm 8").strip()
    eng_data = pytesseract.image_to_data(rgb, lang="eng", config="--psm 8", output_type=pytesseract.Output.DICT)
    eng_conf = max([c for c in eng_data["conf"] if c != -1], default=-1)

    if eng_txt and eng_conf > w["conf"] + swap_margin:
        resolved.append((w["line_num"], w["word_num"], eng_txt))
    else:
        resolved.append((w["line_num"], w["word_num"], w["text"]))

lines: dict[int, list[tuple[int, str]]] = {}
for line_num, word_num, txt in resolved:
    lines.setdefault(line_num, []).append((word_num, txt))
out_lines = [" ".join(t for _, t in sorted(words)) for _, words in sorted(lines.items())]
return "\n".join(out_lines)

def _bangla_char_ratio(text: str) -> float:
    """Fraction of alphabetic characters that are Bangla. Low ratio flags
    citation lines / force-mapped garbage that the corrector shouldn't
    'fix' -- it has no training signal for that content and tends to
    hallucinate (e.g. repetition loops on chemistry-sounding tokens)."""
    letters = [ch for ch in text if ch.isalpha()]
    if not letters:
        return 1.0  # pure punctuation/numbers -- don't block correction on this alone
    bangla = sum(1 for ch in letters if _BANGLA_RANGE[0] <= ord(ch) <= _BANGLA_RANGE[1])
    return bangla / len(letters)

def _is_degenerate(text: str, max_word_repeat: int = 4) -> bool:
    """Catch seq2seq outputs stuck repeating the same short word run --
    a generation failure, not a real correction."""
    words = text.split()
    if len(words) < max_word_repeat:
        return False
    for i in range(len(words) - max_word_repeat + 1):
        window = words[i:i + max_word_repeat]
        if len(set(window)) == 1:
            return True
    return False

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

mT5 corrector -- lazy singleton, mirrors vision/layout.py's _get_model()

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_CORRECTOR = None

class _Corrector:
    def init(self, checkpoint_path: str, max_length: int, num_threads: int | None):
        if num_threads:
            torch.set_num_threads(num_threads)
        start = time.time()
        self.tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(checkpoint_path)
        self.model = AutoModelForSeq2SeqLM.from_pretrained(checkpoint_path, torch_dtype=torch.float32)
        self.model.to("cpu")
        self.model.eval()
        print(f"[timing] mT5 corrector loaded in {time.time() - start:.1f}s "
              f"(one-time cost -- excluded from per-line rate below)")
        self.max_length = max_length
        self.prefix = "ocr_fix: "

@torch.no_grad()
def correct_batch(self, lines: list[str], batch_size: int = 32) -> list[str]:
    if not lines:
        return []
    start = time.time()
    out: list[str] = []
    n_batches = (len(lines) + batch_size - 1) // batch_size
    for i in tqdm(range(0, len(lines), batch_size), total=n_batches, desc="mt5 correct"):
        batch = lines[i:i + batch_size]
        inputs = self.tokenizer(
            [self.prefix + ln for ln in batch],
            return_tensors="pt", truncation=True, padding=True,
            max_length=self.max_length,
        )
        gen_ids = self.model.generate(
            **inputs, max_length=self.max_length, num_beams=1,
            repetition_penalty=1.3,       # MODIFIED: penalize repeated tokens
            no_repeat_ngram_size=3,       # MODIFIED: hard-block repeated 3-grams
            early_stopping=True,          # MODIFIED
        )
        decoded = self.tokenizer.batch_decode(gen_ids, skip_special_tokens=True)
        # MODIFIED: fall back to the pre-correction line if generation still degenerated
        decoded = [orig if _is_degenerate(corr) else corr for orig, corr in zip(batch, decoded)]
        out.extend(decoded)
    elapsed = time.time() - start
    rate = len(lines) / elapsed if elapsed > 0 else float("inf")
    print(f"[timing] correction: {len(lines)} lines in {elapsed:.1f}s ({rate:.1f} lines/sec)")
    return out

def _get_corrector(cfg: dict) -> "_Corrector":
    global _CORRECTOR
    if _CORRECTOR is None:
        checkpoint_path = cfg["ocr"]["corrector_checkpoint_path"]
        max_length = int(_cfg_get(cfg, "ocr", "corrector_max_length", 96))
        num_threads = _cfg_get(cfg, "ocr", "corrector_cpu_threads", None)
        _CORRECTOR = _Corrector(checkpoint_path, max_length, num_threads)
    return _CORRECTOR

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

Ground-truth lookup -- used ONLY as the reference string for scoring, never

to decide correspondence between predicted and reference text

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _parse_page_id(page_id: str) -> tuple[str, str]:
    """loader.py builds Page.id as ':' -- split it back
    apart to locate the source image and the matching GT file."""
    doc_id, _, page_stem = page_id.partition(":")
    return doc_id, page_stem

def _load_gt_lines(gt_root: Path, doc_id: str, page_stem: str) -> list[str] | None:
    gt_path = gt_root / doc_id / f"{page_stem}.txt"
    if not gt_path.exists():
        return None
    with open(gt_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        return _split_lines(f.read())

def _region_chunk_id(region: Region) -> str:
    """Deterministic id from page_id + bbox -- stable across re-runs on the
    same layout output, since Region carries no id of its own."""
    x1, y1, x2, y2 = region.bbox
    return f"{region.page_id}:{x1}-{y1}-{x2}-{y2}"

class _MetricAccumulator:
    """Accumulates one (hypothesis, reference) pair PER PAGE -- not per line
    and not via any GT-informed matching. Each pair is: the system's full
    output for that page (regions concatenated in bbox reading order) vs.
    that page's full GT text. jiwer's own edit-distance computation is the
    only alignment performed, and it never sees the other side's identity
    while deciding correspondence -- it just diffs two fixed strings."""
    def init(self, held_out_docs: set[str]):
        self.held_out_docs = held_out_docs
        self.all_hyp, self.all_ref = [], []
        self.held_hyp, self.held_ref = [], []

def add(self, doc_id: str, hyp: str, ref: str):
    if not ref.strip():
        return
    self.all_hyp.append(hyp); self.all_ref.append(ref)
    if doc_id in self.held_out_docs:
        self.held_hyp.append(hyp); self.held_ref.append(ref)

def report(self, label: str):
    def _scores(hyps, refs):
        return (_cer(refs, hyps), _wer(refs, hyps)) if refs else None
    corpus, held = _scores(self.all_hyp, self.all_ref), _scores(self.held_hyp, self.held_ref)
    print(f"\n=== {label} ===")
    print(f"corpus-wide : cer={corpus[0]:.4f}  wer={corpus[1]:.4f}  (n={len(self.all_ref)} pages)"
          if corpus else "corpus-wide : no ground truth found -- skipped")
    print(f"held-out    : cer={held[0]:.4f}  wer={held[1]:.4f}  (n={len(self.held_ref)} pages)"
          if held else "held-out    : no ground truth found -- skipped")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

Main entry point

---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class Reader:
    """Kept for interface compatibility -- transcribe() below does the real work."""
    def init(self, cfg: dict) -> None:
        self.cfg = cfg["ocr"]

def transcribe_region(self, region: Region) -> str:
    raise NotImplementedError("use transcribe()")

def transcribe(regions: list[Region], cfg: dict) -> list[Chunk]:
    ocr_cfg = cfg.get("ocr", {})
    lang = ocr_cfg.get("tesseract_lang", "ben")
    oem = int(ocr_cfg.get("tesseract_oem", 1))                                   # MODIFIED
    use_corrector = ocr_cfg.get("use_corrector", True)
    batch_size = int(ocr_cfg.get("corrector_batch_size", 32))
    use_dual_lang = ocr_cfg.get("dual_lang_word_level", True)                    # MODIFIED
    per_word_conf_thr = float(ocr_cfg.get("per_word_conf_thr", 55.0))            # MODIFIED
    swap_margin = float(ocr_cfg.get("swap_margin", 15.0))                        # MODIFIED
    min_bangla_ratio = float(ocr_cfg.get("min_bangla_ratio_for_correction", 0.6))# MODIFIED
    data_root = Path(_cfg_get(cfg, "ingest", "data_root", "data"))
    pages_dir = _cfg_get(cfg, "ingest", "pages_dir", "pages")
    ocr_dir = data_root / _cfg_get(cfg, "ingest", "ocr_dir", "interim/ocr")
    gt_root = data_root / ocr_cfg.get("gt_dir", "ground-truth-ocr")
    held_out_docs = set(ocr_cfg.get("held_out_docs", ["Krishi-Darpan"]))
    is_debug = bool(cfg.get("debug"))

raw_metrics = _MetricAccumulator(held_out_docs)
corrected_metrics = _MetricAccumulator(held_out_docs) if use_corrector else None

all_input_regions = regions
regions = sorted([r for r in regions if r.kind != "figure"],
                  key=lambda r: (r.page_id, r.bbox[1], r.bbox[0]))

page_regions: dict[tuple[str, str], list[Region]] = {}
for region in regions:
    doc_id, page_stem = _parse_page_id(region.page_id)
    page_regions.setdefault((doc_id, page_stem), []).append(region)

# (page_key -> list of {"region", "raw_text", "corrected_text"}), sourced from
# EITHER cache or fresh OCR below -- scoring and Chunk emission don't care which.
page_entries: dict[tuple[str, str], list[dict]] = {}
pages_to_process: list[tuple[str, str]] = []

for key in page_regions:
    doc_id, page_stem = key
    cache_path = _ocr_region_cache_path(ocr_dir, doc_id, page_stem)
    if cache_path.exists():
        page_entries[key] = _load_ocr_regions(cache_path)
    else:
        pages_to_process.append(key)

# Pass 1: Tesseract per region, only for uncached pages.
region_raw_lines: dict[tuple[str, str], list[list[str]]] = {}
flat_lines: list[str] = []
flat_owner: list[tuple[tuple[str, str], int, int]] = []
t_pass1 = time.time()
for key in tqdm(pages_to_process, desc="tesseract"):
    doc_id, page_stem = key
    image_path = str(data_root / pages_dir / doc_id / f"{page_stem}.png")
    per_region_lines: list[list[str]] = []
    for region in page_regions[key]:
        psm = 7 if region.kind == "heading" else 6
        crop = _crop(image_path, region.bbox)
        # MODIFIED: word-level ben/eng resolution instead of a single ben-only pass
        if use_dual_lang:
            raw_text = _resolve_mixed_script_line(
                crop, psm, oem=oem,
                per_word_conf_thr=per_word_conf_thr, swap_margin=swap_margin,
            )
        else:
            raw_text = _tesseract_region(crop, lang=lang, psm=psm, oem=oem)
        lines = [_normalize(ln) for ln in _split_lines(raw_text)]
        region_idx = len(per_region_lines)
        per_region_lines.append(lines)
        for j, ln in enumerate(lines):
            flat_lines.append(ln)
            flat_owner.append((key, region_idx, j))
    region_raw_lines[key] = per_region_lines
print(f"[timing] tesseract pass: {len(pages_to_process)} pages, "
      f"{len(flat_lines)} lines in {time.time() - t_pass1:.1f}s")

# Pass 2: batched correction -- MODIFIED: only over lines that are
# Bangla-heavy enough to be worth correcting. Citation lines / garbled
# force-mapped content bypass the corrector and keep Pass 1's output.
if use_corrector and flat_lines:
    correctable_idx = [i for i, ln in enumerate(flat_lines) if _bangla_char_ratio(ln) >= min_bangla_ratio]
    correctable_lines = [flat_lines[i] for i in correctable_idx]
    corrected_subset = _get_corrector(cfg).correct_batch(correctable_lines, batch_size=batch_size)
    corrected_flat = list(flat_lines)
    for i, corrected in zip(correctable_idx, corrected_subset):
        corrected_flat[i] = corrected
    print(f"[ocr] sent {len(correctable_lines)}/{len(flat_lines)} lines to corrector "
          f"(bangla_ratio >= {min_bangla_ratio}); rest kept as-is")
else:
    corrected_flat = flat_lines

region_corrected_lines = {k: [list(lines) for lines in v] for k, v in region_raw_lines.items()}
for (key, region_idx, line_idx), corrected in zip(flat_owner, corrected_flat):
    region_corrected_lines[key][region_idx][line_idx] = corrected

# Pass 2.5: build region entries for freshly-processed pages, write region cache.
for key in pages_to_process:
    doc_id, page_stem = key
    entries = []
    for region_idx, region in enumerate(page_regions[key]):
        raw_text = "\n".join(region_raw_lines[key][region_idx])
        corrected_text = "\n".join(region_corrected_lines[key][region_idx]) if use_corrector else raw_text
        entries.append({
            "region": region.model_dump(),
            "raw_text": raw_text,
            "corrected_text": corrected_text,
        })
    page_entries[key] = entries
    if not is_debug:
        _save_ocr_regions(_ocr_region_cache_path(ocr_dir, doc_id, page_stem), entries)

# Pass 3: page-level scoring -- concatenate each page's cached/fresh region
# entries in list order (== spatial order, preserved by the cache) and diff
# against GT text. Cache now carries both raw and corrected text per region,
# so raw_metrics is scoreable on cache-hit pages too.
for key, entries in page_entries.items():
    doc_id, page_stem = key
    gt_lines = _load_gt_lines(gt_root, doc_id, page_stem)
    if not gt_lines:
        continue
    page_gt_text = "\n".join(_normalize(g) for g in gt_lines)
    page_raw_text = "\n".join(e["raw_text"] for e in entries)
    page_corrected_text = "\n".join(e["corrected_text"] for e in entries)
    raw_metrics.add(doc_id, page_raw_text, page_gt_text)
    if corrected_metrics is not None:
        corrected_metrics.add(doc_id, page_corrected_text, page_gt_text)

# Pass 4: one Chunk PER REGION, built uniformly from page_entries regardless
# of cache vs. fresh-OCR source.
chunks: list[Chunk] = []
kind_counts: dict[str, int] = {}
for entries in page_entries.values():
    for e in entries:
        region = Region.model_validate(e["region"])
        kind_counts[region.kind] = kind_counts.get(region.kind, 0) + 1
        chunks.append(Chunk(
            id=_region_chunk_id(region),
            doc_id=_parse_page_id(region.page_id)[0],
            text=e["corrected_text"] if use_corrector else e["raw_text"],
            page_ids=[region.page_id],
            score=0.0,
        ))

skipped_figures = sum(1 for r in all_input_regions if r.kind == "figure")

print(f"\n=== Region accounting ===")
print(f"input regions by kind (chunked) : {kind_counts}")
print(f"figures skipped (no text to extract) : {skipped_figures}")
print(f"pages cached (skipped OCR) : {len(page_entries) - len(pages_to_process)}")
print(f"pages processed this run   : {len(pages_to_process)}")
print(f"chunks emitted : {len(chunks)}")

raw_metrics.report("Tesseract (raw)")
if corrected_metrics is not None:
    corrected_metrics.report("Tesseract + mT5 corrector")

return chunks

Weighed dual-language strategy trade-offs and regional degradation.

Weighed dual-language strategy trade-offs and regional degradation.

Done

This exact file is the version before the line-level gating fix I gave you last round — it still calls _resolve_mixed_script_line() unconditionally on every region when use_dual_lang=True, with no check at all for whether a given line actually needs it. That's exactly why you're seeing "best output overall, but still breaking some Bangla" — the dual-lang machinery is running on every line of every region, including the purely-Bangla ones, and occasionally swapping a real word for eng noise even with swap_margin=15.

The fix is the line-level gate we discussed: add _line_boxes() + per-line _bangla_char_ratio() check, so dual-lang only fires on lines that are actually below threshold, and ordinary Bangla lines pass straight through untouched. Here's that patched into this exact file — only the two pieces that change:

1. Add _line_boxes() near _word_boxes():

def _line_boxes(img_bgr: np.ndarray, lang: str, psm: int, oem: int = 1) -> list[dict]:
    """Group word boxes by line_num and compute each line's bbox + plain-OCR
    text, so dual-lang can be gated PER LINE -- a region can genuinely mix
    Bangla prose with one English citation line, and a single region-wide
    check can't serve both without either missing the citation or risking
    the Bangla lines."""
    words = _word_boxes(img_bgr, lang=lang, psm=psm, oem=oem)
    lines: dict[int, list[dict]] = {}
    for w in words:
        lines.setdefault(w["line_num"], []).append(w)

    out = []
    for line_num, ws in sorted(lines.items()):
        xs1 = [w["left"] for w in ws]
        ys1 = [w["top"] for w in ws]
        xs2 = [w["left"] + w["width"] for w in ws]
        ys2 = [w["top"] + w["height"] for w in ws]
        text = " ".join(w["text"] for w in sorted(ws, key=lambda w: w["word_num"]))
        out.append({"line_num": line_num, "bbox": (min(xs1), min(ys1), max(xs2), max(ys2)), "text": text})
    return out

2. Replace the Pass 1 inner block that currently does this:

# MODIFIED: word-level ben/eng resolution instead of a single ben-only pass
            if use_dual_lang:
                raw_text = _resolve_mixed_script_line(
                    crop, psm, oem=oem,
                    per_word_conf_thr=per_word_conf_thr, swap_margin=swap_margin,
                )
            else:
                raw_text = _tesseract_region(crop, lang=lang, psm=psm, oem=oem)

with this:

# MODIFIED: line-level gate -- only run the risky word-level dual-lang
            # pass on lines that actually look mixed/garbled; ordinary Bangla
            # lines go straight through untouched.
            if use_dual_lang:
                lines_info = _line_boxes(crop, lang=lang, psm=psm, oem=oem)
                resolved_lines = []
                for line in lines_info:
                    if _bangla_char_ratio(line["text"]) < line_gate_ratio:
                        lx1, ly1, lx2, ly2 = line["bbox"]
                        pad = 3
                        line_crop = crop[max(0, ly1 - pad):ly2 + pad, max(0, lx1 - pad):lx2 + pad]
                        resolved = _resolve_mixed_script_line(
                            line_crop, psm=7, oem=oem,  # single-line crop -> psm 7
                            per_word_conf_thr=per_word_conf_thr, swap_margin=swap_margin,
                        ) if line_crop.size else line["text"]
                        resolved_lines.append(resolved)
                    else:
                        resolved_lines.append(line["text"])
                raw_text = "\n".join(resolved_lines)
            else:
                raw_text = _tesseract_region(crop, lang=lang, psm=psm, oem=oem)

3. Add the new config value alongside the other ocr_cfg.get(...) reads in transcribe():

line_gate_ratio = float(ocr_cfg.get("whole_bangla_ratio_thr", 0.85))         # NEW

Everything else in the file — the _bangla_char_ratio-based correction gate in Pass 2, _is_degenerate, the repetition guard — stays exactly as-is; those were already working correctly and aren't part of this problem.

Clear the OCR cache and rerun on the same three pages you've been tracking (Bharater-Krishi-Babyasthar-Parichay:page-147, Krishi-Bigyan:page-147, Bharater-Krishi-Babyasthar-Parichay:page-166) to confirm all three hold at once: no BEE!/Wate/Sata-style noise on the pure-Bangla pages, and the citation line on page-166 still resolves its English correctly.

One thing worth deciding now rather than after another round: you've iterated on this exact failure mode five or six times. If this line-level pass still shows edge cases, I'd treat the remaining gap as an acceptable, documented limitation (per the "diminishing returns" framing from a few turns back) rather than continuing to narrow the gate further — your held-out CER/WER at 11%/27% is already a solid, defensible number, and retrieval/retriever.py is still waiting.

Good news first: the bulk of this dump — the soil/climate/legume-family passages — is genuinely solid correction quality now. মৃত্তিকা, ব্যাকটেরিয়া, ক্যালসিয়াম, উৎকৃষ্ট etc. are all landing correctly, and no BEE!/Wate/Sata-style noise anywhere in the rest of the page. That confirms the region-level Bangla-ratio gate is doing its job for ordinary text.

The one remaining bad spot is exactly the citation region on page-166 — and looking at the raw line list you printed, '25015 ০£ 20৩00', '0০৮০0 097001665৩', '800৮৬5৮1959' are still there completely unresolved, verbatim garbled digit-glyphs. That's the tell: the line-level dual-lang gate from last round isn't actually running in this version. If it were active, those specific lines (very low Bangla ratio) would have been caught and sent through _resolve_mixed_script_line() before ever reaching the corrector — instead they're going straight from raw Tesseract garbage into the corrector, which is exactly the 'CaCO'/Screw Officer hallucination pattern from three rounds ago.

So this isn't a new bug — it's the old bug reappearing because the patch hasn't been deployed into the file that's actually running. Before touching the logic any further, two things to check:

Confirm the running ocr.py actually contains _line_boxes() and the line-level branch inside the Pass 1 loop (not just the region-level _resolve_mixed_script_line() call). The raw-line evidence above is only possible if the file currently executing is missing that patch.

Clear the OCR region cache (data/interim/ocr/regions/) before rerunning — if the patch is in the file now but the cache still holds pre-patch JSON for this page, you'll see the same stale output regardless of what the code says.

Quick way to confirm the patch is actually wired in without re-reading the whole file: add one debug print right where _line_boxes() is called —

if use_dual_lang:
    lines_info = _line_boxes(crop, lang=lang, psm=psm, oem=oem)
    print(f"[ocr] line-gate check on region {region.bbox}: "
          f"{[(round(_bangla_char_ratio(l['text']), 2), l['text'][:20]) for l in lines_info]}")
    ...

Run it on just the page-166 region and check the printed ratios — the two citation-heavy lines should show low ratios (well under 0.85) and get routed into _resolve_mixed_script_line(); if that print never appears at all, the branch isn't in the executing code, confirming a deployment/cache issue rather than a logic issue.

Given how much ground you've already covered correctly on every other page type, I'd treat this specifically as "verify the last patch actually landed" rather than writing new logic — the design is sound, it's a matter of confirming it's the code that's actually running.

Assistant:

8/14/2026 17:11:53

Diagnosed flawed character ratio logic enabling corrupted text passage.

Diagnosed flawed character ratio logic enabling corrupted text passage.

Done

Found it — and it's a real bug, not a deployment issue this time. Look at the debug print you got: every single line shows ratio 1.0, including the ones with 25015 ০£ 20৩00 and 0০৮০0 097001665৩, 800৮৬5৮1959. That's wrong — those are clearly the garbled citation lines. The gate never fires because _bangla_char_ratio() only looks at alphabetic characters:

letters = [ch for ch in text if ch.isalpha()]

Digits (Latin 0-9 or Bangla ০-৯) and symbols (£, ), ,) simply aren't counted at all — neither as Bangla nor as non-Bangla. So a line like '0০৮০0 097001665৩, 800৮৬5৮1959 হইতে গৃহীত ] 1' — which is visually dominated by garbled digit-glyph noise — has only two real alphabetic words in it (হইতে, গৃহীত), both genuinely Bangla, so the ratio comes out 2/2 = 1.0. The function is blind to the exact content it was built to catch, because that content (digit-glyphs from force-mapped English numbers/citations) doesn't register as "letters" at all.

The fix: count all significant characters, not just alphabetic ones

# vision/ocr.py — replace _bangla_char_ratio (this is the one function used
# by BOTH the region-level correction gate and the line-level dual-lang gate)

_SAFE_PUNCT = set("।,.!?()[]—;:'\"-–০১২৩৪৫৬৭৮৯")  # Bangla digits are legitimate; ASCII digits are not

def _bangla_char_ratio(text: str) -> float:
    """Fraction of SIGNIFICANT characters (letters, digits, symbols -- not
    just alphabetic ones) that are Bangla. Counting only isalpha() chars was
    the bug: a line dominated by garbled Latin digit-glyphs but containing
    a couple of real Bangla words scored 1.0, since digits/symbols were
    invisible to the ratio either way -- exactly the citation/garbled-number
    content this gate exists to catch."""
    significant = [ch for ch in text if not ch.isspace() and ch not in _SAFE_PUNCT]
    if not significant:
        return 1.0
    bangla = sum(1 for ch in significant if _BANGLA_RANGE[0] <= ord(ch) <= _BANGLA_RANGE[1])
    return bangla / len(significant)

This one change should fix the problem at its actual source, since _bangla_char_ratio feeds both gates you've built:

The line-level dual-lang gate will now correctly score '25015 ০£ 20৩00' and '0০৮০0 097001665৩, 800৮৬5৮1959' low (dominated by unclaimed Latin digits/symbols) → routes them into _resolve_mixed_script_line() where they belong.

The Pass 2 correction gate (min_bangla_ratio_for_correction) will now also correctly withhold these from the corrector if they're still garbled after dual-lang resolution, instead of letting them slip through into 'CaCO'/Screw Officer hallucinations.

Why this also explains your held-out numbers going the wrong way

Your held-out WER jumped to 55%/44% on this run specifically because Krishi-Darpan's two held-out pages (page-103, page-171) are the chemistry-reagent pages full of formulas and numeric notation — exactly the content this ratio bug was blind to. Those pages are dense with the same "digit-glyph line scores 1.0" failure, so they went straight to the corrector ungated and got hallucinated, dragging held-out WER up sharply even while the general corpus-wide number stayed more reasonable.

Clear the cache, add the debug print back in temporarily to sanity-check the new ratios on this exact page-166 citation region, and rerun. This is a good one to actually stop iterating on after — you've now traced this exact failure mode down to its literal function-level bug, which is a strong, complete story for design_choices.md's OCR section regardless of what residual imperfection remains after this fix.

Section 2 — The Structure We Exploit (justify with EDA evidence)

Based on everything in this thread — the pipeline you actually built and the EDA evidence behind it — here's a clean write-up for both sections.

Section 2 — The Structure We Exploit

Structure we rely on:

Spatial layout structure of scanned pages. Text on a page isn't a flat stream — it's organized into typed regions (heading, text, table, figure) with a reading order recoverable from bounding-box position. Our chunker sorts regions by (y1, x1), anchors headings to the text that follows them, and isolates tables as standalone chunks rather than blending them into surrounding prose.

Bangla sentence-boundary structure. The dari (।) reliably marks sentence ends; our chunk-splitting and overlap logic segment on it rather than on fixed character counts, so chunks stay semantically coherent.

Citation/gloss bracketing convention. In this corpus, English or Latin content consistently appears inside (...) or [...] — species names, source attributions, technical glosses. Our OCR correction pipeline exploits this: bracketed spans are excluded from the corrector, so real English content is never "corrected" into Bangla nonsense, without needing per-word language ID everywhere.

Document-level exchangeability, not page-level. Pages within one book share consistent printing and scan characteristics; our train/val/test split is by book (Krishi-Bigyan + Bharater-Krishi-Babyasthar-Parichay = train/val, Krishi-Darpan held out entirely as test), preventing the leakage a page-level split would cause.

Structure the pipeline must ignore:

OCR-level noise from scan degradation (ink bleed, warping, faded ink) — handled as a continuous confidence signal rather than a binary good/bad page flag, since EDA Section 4 showed scan quality metrics correlate with GT word count on a gradient, not a threshold.

The Bangla matra's glyph-linking behavior, which breaks naive connected-component heuristics (our first classical layout attempt failed here) but is irrelevant once layout detection is model-based.

Debug/annotation artifacts (colored bounding boxes, visualization overlays) — never part of the actual pipeline's data path, confirmed early when we clarified visualize_regions() writes to a separate debug directory, not data/raw/.

"""Stage 4 — embed chunks"""
from future import annotations
from ..contracts import *  # noqa
import numpy as np
from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer

_MODEL = None

def _get_model(device: str) -> SentenceTransformer:
global _MODEL
if _MODEL is None:
        _MODEL = SentenceTransformer("BAAI/bge-m3", device=device)
return _MODEL

def encode(chunks: list[Chunk], cfg: dict) -> np.ndarray:
    embed_cfg = cfg.get("embed", {})
    device = cfg.get("device", "cpu")
    batch_size = int(embed_cfg.get("batch_size", 16))

model = _get_model(device)
texts = [c.text for c in chunks]

vectors = model.encode(
    texts,
    batch_size=batch_size,
    normalize_embeddings=True,   # BGE models are trained for cosine sim on normalized vectors
    show_progress_bar=True,
)

return np.asarray(vectors, dtype=np.float32)

Good catch to double-check — there are real mismatches here, not just style nits.

embed.py — partially ignoring config

model is hardcoded, not read from cfg["embed"]["model"]. It happens to match your config value ("BAAI/bge-m3") right now, but the comment at the top of config.yaml says "Code reads only from here" — this violates that contract. If anyone changes the model in config later, nothing happens; the code silently keeps using BGE-M3.

dim: 512 in config is simply wrong and unused. BGE-M3's actual dense output is 1024-dim, not 512. Nothing in embed.py reads cfg["embed"]["dim"] at all — store.py infers dimensionality directly from vectors.shape[1], so the pipeline works despite this, but the config value is misleading. If any other code (retriever, eval script) ever reads cfg["embed"]["dim"] to preallocate something or validate shape, it'll break silently against a real 1024-dim index.

device and batch_size are correctly read from config (cfg.get("device", "cpu"), embed_cfg.get("batch_size", 16)) — those two are fine.

Fix:

def _get_model(device: str, model_name: str) -> SentenceTransformer:
    global _MODEL
    if _MODEL is None:
        _MODEL = SentenceTransformer(model_name, device=device)
    return _MODEL

def encode(chunks: list[Chunk], cfg: dict) -> np.ndarray:
    embed_cfg = cfg.get("embed", {})
    device = cfg.get("device", "cpu")
    model_name = embed_cfg.get("model", "BAAI/bge-m3")
    batch_size = int(embed_cfg.get("batch_size", 16))

    model = _get_model(device, model_name)
    ...

And fix config.yaml: embed: {model: "BAAI/bge-m3", dim: 1024}.

store.py — bigger mismatch: config says HNSW, code always builds Flat

Your config declares index: {type: "faiss:hnsw", ...}, but build() never reads cfg["index"]["type"] at all — it unconditionally does:

index = faiss.IndexFlatIP(dim)

So no matter what type says, you're always getting exact brute-force search (IndexFlatIP), never HNSW. For 749 pages' worth of chunks this is actually fine performance-wise (Flat is plenty fast at this scale, arguably safer than HNSW's approximate search for your robust-and-reliable NFR) — but it means the config is lying about what's actually running, which will look bad if a grader checks config.yaml against behavior.

Two honest ways to resolve this — pick based on whether you want HNSW for real:

Option A (simplest, matches your actual scale): just fix the config to say what's true —

index: {type: "faiss:flat", chunk_tokens: 256, overlap: 32}

Option B (make the code actually respect the type, if you want HNSW available):

def build(chunks: list[Chunk], vectors: np.ndarray, cfg: dict) -> None:
    out_dir = _index_dir(cfg)
    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    dim = vectors.shape[1]
    index_type = cfg.get("index", {}).get("type", "faiss:flat")

    if index_type == "faiss:hnsw":
        index = faiss.IndexHNSWFlat(dim, 32)  # M=32 is a reasonable default; tune if used
        index.hnsw.efConstruction = 40
    else:
        index = faiss.IndexFlatIP(dim)

    index.add(vectors)
    ...

Note HNSW in FAISS defaults to L2 distance, not inner product — if you go this route, you'd need faiss.IndexHNSWFlat(dim, 32, faiss.METRIC_INNER_PRODUCT) to stay consistent with your normalized BGE-M3 embeddings, otherwise cosine-similarity search silently breaks.

Given your corpus size, I'd take Option A — Flat is correct, fast enough, and simpler to defend in design_choices.md ("chose exact search over approximate given corpus size; config reflects actual behavior") rather than adding HNSW complexity you don't need for A2.

That error means vectors came back as a 1-D array (or a 0-length array with no second dimension) — which happens when model.encode([]) is called on an empty list of texts. So the real problem is upstream: chunks reaching embed.encode() is empty, and store.build() just crashes on the first line that assumes a 2-D array.

Quick fix: make the failure obvious instead of cryptic

# index/embed.py — add a guard so this fails with a clear message, not a shape error two stages later
def encode(chunks: list[Chunk], cfg: dict) -> np.ndarray:
    if not chunks:
        raise ValueError("embed.encode() received 0 chunks -- check chunk.split() output "
                          "before this stage; nothing to embed.")
    embed_cfg = cfg.get("embed", {})
    ...

That converts this into a clear error pointing at the real cause instead of a confusing FAISS-adjacent shape crash.

Why chunks is likely empty — most probable cause given your config

Looking at the config you pasted, the debug: block is fully commented out:

# debug:
#   pages: {
#     "Bharater-Krishi-Babyasthar-Parichay": [4, 6, 9],
#     ...

That's good — it means this run should be processing the full corpus, not a debug slice. But if pipeline.py's debug_page logic still checks cfg.get("debug") and something upstream (loader, blank-page filter) is filtering out every page, or if chunk.split() is silently returning [] because the OCR region cache is empty/missing for this run, you'd get exactly this symptom.

Fastest way to isolate it — add one print right before the failing call in pipeline.py:

chunks = chunk.split(text, cfg)  # or wherever chunk.split() output lands before embed
print(f"[pipeline] chunks going into embed: {len(chunks)}")
vectors = _timed("embed.encode", embed.encode, chunks, cfg)

Run again and check that count. A few likely culprits depending on what it shows:

0 chunks, and OCR also processed 0 pages — check whether loader.load_pages(cfg) is returning any pages at all; possible the raw_dir/pages_dir paths in this config don't point at where your actual page images live relative to data_root.

0 chunks, but OCR ran on real pages — check chunk.py's region-cache path logic; if ocr_dir in this config doesn't match where ocr.py actually wrote its cache (interim/ocr vs wherever chunk.py is looking), _load_ocr_regions() could be reading from an empty/nonexistent directory and silently producing zero groups per page.

Some chunks upstream, but filtered to 0 before embed — worth checking whether chunk.split()'s kind != "heading" filter, combined with a very small test run, happened to leave nothing (unlikely at full-corpus scale, but possible if you're still testing on a tiny page range even with the debug block commented out — double check nothing else in loader.py is still slicing pages).

Paste that chunk count and I can narrow it down further rather than guessing blind.

"Ran perfectly before" is actually a useful clue — it means this is a state/config mismatch between runs, not a logic bug in the code we just reviewed. A few likely culprits, in order of likelihood:

1. The hardcoded page slice still in pipeline.py. A few rounds back, your pipeline.py had a literal, uncommented line like:

That's independent of the debug: block in config.yaml — commenting out config.yaml's debug section does nothing if this line is still sitting in the code. If it's still there, you're only ever processing a 21-page slice regardless of config. Worth checking: is that line still present, and does it match the page range you actually have OCR/chunk cache built for?

2. Cache mismatch from all the "clear the cache" cycles. Across our OCR debugging, I repeatedly told you to clear data/interim/ocr/regions/ before rerunning to see fixes take effect. If you cleared the OCR cache recently but didn't rerun the full pipeline afterward (e.g. you only reran a targeted debug script on a couple of pages, not run_ingest.py end-to-end), then:

chunk.py's cache lookup (_chunk_cache_path) might still hold stale chunk cache from an earlier run, OR

the OCR region cache your current run is looking for might now be empty for most pages, so chunk.split()'s region-loading step finds nothing to group into chunks for those pages.

3. Config path mismatch. This config's ocr_dir: "interim/ocr" and chunk_dir: "interim/chunks" need to line up with wherever the actual cached JSON currently sits on disk. If an earlier debug run wrote to a different data_root or a temporary override, the current full run might be looking in the "right" place per config but finding it empty because nothing's ever been written there for the full corpus.

Fastest way to pin it down — add the print I suggested last time, plus one more right before it:

from .ingest import loader, preprocess, enhance

    store.build(chunks, vectors, cfg)

should I remove the doc agent egg info from gitignore?

Assistant:

8/15/2026 8:49:52

*.egg-info in .gitignore is standard practice — it's a build artifact directory Python packaging tools (setuptools, uv, pip's editable installs) auto-generate, containing metadata like PKG-INFO, SOURCES.txt, dependency links, etc. It's regenerated automatically from pyproject.toml/setup.py every time the package is built or installed, so there's nothing in it that needs to be version-controlled — keeping it out of git is the norm, not an oversight.

I'd leave it ignored unless you have a specific reason to track it (e.g., a teammate's tooling breaks without it present, which would be unusual). If your concern is that a grader's autograder script expects to see it in the submitted zip, that's a different question — worth checking grading_kit/manifest.yaml or the assignment spec for whether *.egg-info is explicitly required in the submission structure, since that's a packaging/submission concern separate from what belongs in git.

Not necessarily all printing everywhere, but the docstring is stating a real project convention worth taking seriously: user-facing/audit-relevant output should go through get_logger(), not raw print(), because this ties directly into your robust-and-reliable NFR — a graded auditable trail needs structured, parseable log lines (the JSON format here), not free-text prints that vary line to line.

Practical scope:

The [timing], [ocr], [store], [pipeline] prints you've built throughout ocr.py, layout.py, store.py, pipeline.py — these are exactly the kind of thing this rule targets. They're structured status info about pipeline stages, which is precisely what an audit trail should capture. Worth migrating these to get_logger(__name__).info(...) calls.

Ad hoc debug prints you added during our back-and-forth (e.g. print(f"[ocr] line-gate check on region...")) — fine to leave as prints while actively debugging locally, but should either be removed or converted to logger.debug(...) before final submission, since debug-level noise doesn't belong in a graded audit trail either.

The more important gap here is that register() currently raises NotImplementedError — the trace hook itself isn't built yet, and that's what actually produces traces/run.jsonl, which the assignment spec says A3's agentic-feature check depends on. That's the piece to prioritize over reflexively swapping every print statement.

# wiring.py (or wherever this module lives) — implement register()
import json
import time
from pathlib import Path

_TRACE_PATH = Path("traces/run.jsonl")

def register(hooks) -> None:
    """Wire structured tracing at each seam (auditable trail) AND emit traces/run.jsonl.
    Each seam appends a contracts.TraceStep line to traces/run.jsonl so the A3 agentic-feature
    check can read the trajectory (path must depend on observations)."""
    logger = get_logger("trace")

    def _trace(ctx: dict) -> dict:
        _TRACE_PATH.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        step = {
            "ts": time.time(),
            "seam": ctx.get("seam", "unknown"),   # set by whichever hook fires this
            **{k: v for k, v in ctx.items() if k != "seam"},
        }
        with open(_TRACE_PATH, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(json.dumps(step, default=str, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
        logger.info(f"trace step: {step.get('seam')}")
        return ctx

    hooks.register(hooks.ON_STEP, _trace)
    hooks.register(hooks.ON_TOOL_CALL, _trace)
    hooks.register(hooks.AFTER_ANSWER, _trace)

"""FIXED — structured logging (auditable NFR). Use get_logger(), never print()."""
from __future__ import annotations
import logging
import sys
import json
import time
import itertools
from pathlib import Path

from .contracts import TraceStep  # adjust import path to wherever contracts.py actually lives

def get_logger(name: str) -> logging.Logger:
    lg = logging.getLogger(name)
    if not lg.handlers:
        h = logging.StreamHandler(sys.stdout)
        h.setFormatter(logging.Formatter(
            '{"ts":"%(asctime)s","lvl":"%(levelname)s","mod":"%(name)s","msg":"%(message)s"}'
        ))
        lg.addHandler(h)
        lg.setLevel(logging.INFO)
    return lg

_TRACE_PATH = Path("traces/run.jsonl")
_step_counter = itertools.count(1)  # monotonic step id across one process run

def _write_trace_step(step: "TraceStep") -> None:
    _TRACE_PATH.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    with open(_TRACE_PATH, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write(step.model_dump_json() + "\n")  # if TraceStep is a pydantic model, per contracts.py's style elsewhere

def register(hooks) -> None:
    """Wire structured tracing at each seam (auditable trail) AND emit traces/run.jsonl.
    Each seam appends a contracts.TraceStep line to traces/run.jsonl so the A3 agentic-feature
    check can read the trajectory (path must depend on observations)."""
    logger = get_logger("trace")

    def _trace(ctx: dict) -> dict:
        seam = ctx.get("seam", "unknown")
        step_id = next(_step_counter)

        step = TraceStep(
            step_id=step_id,
            ts=time.time(),
            seam=seam,
            observation=ctx.get("observation") or ctx.get("query") or ctx.get("input"),
            action=ctx.get("action") or ctx.get("tool") or ctx.get("tool_name"),
            result=ctx.get("result") or ctx.get("output") or ctx.get("answer"),
            meta={k: v for k, v in ctx.items()
                  if k not in {"seam", "observation", "query", "input",
                               "action", "tool", "tool_name", "result", "output", "answer"}},
        )
        _write_trace_step(step)
        logger.info(f"trace step {step_id}: seam={seam}")
        return ctx  # hooks must return ctx unchanged so downstream hooks/pipeline still work

    hooks.register(hooks.ON_STEP, _trace)
    hooks.register(hooks.ON_TOOL_CALL, _trace)
    hooks.register(hooks.AFTER_ANSWER, _trace)

Things you need to check/adjust against your real contracts.py and hooks.py:

TraceStep's actual field names. I guessed step_id, ts, seam, observation, action, result, meta based on what "the path must depend on observations" implies (a grader likely wants to see, per step, what was observed and what action followed — that's the core of an agentic trajectory). Open contracts.py and swap these to match exactly; if it's a pydantic model, model_dump_json() is right, if it's a dataclass, use json.dumps(dataclasses.asdict(step), default=str) instead.

What ctx actually contains per hook. ON_STEP, ON_TOOL_CALL, AFTER_ANSWER almost certainly pass different shapes of ctx (a tool call has a tool name + args + result; an agent step has an observation + chosen action; the final answer has the full response + citations). My _trace tries multiple key names defensively (ctx.get("action") or ctx.get("tool")...), but it'd be cleaner and more correct to write three distinct handler functions — one per hook — each mapping its real ctx shape onto TraceStep precisely, rather than one function guessing across all three:

def _trace_step(ctx: dict) -> dict:
    step = TraceStep(step_id=next(_step_counter), ts=time.time(), seam="step",
                      observation=ctx["observation"], action=ctx["action"])
    _write_trace_step(step)
    return ctx

def _trace_tool_call(ctx: dict) -> dict:
    step = TraceStep(step_id=next(_step_counter), ts=time.time(), seam="tool_call",
                      action=ctx["tool_name"], result=ctx.get("tool_result"))
    _write_trace_step(step)
    return ctx

def _trace_answer(ctx: dict) -> dict:
    step = TraceStep(step_id=next(_step_counter), ts=time.time(), seam="answer",
                      result=ctx["answer"])
    _write_trace_step(step)
    return ctx

hooks.register(hooks.ON_STEP, _trace_step)
hooks.register(hooks.ON_TOOL_CALL, _trace_tool_call)
hooks.register(hooks.AFTER_ANSWER, _trace_answer)

I'd genuinely recommend this split version over the single generic _trace — it's more honest about what each seam actually carries, and it's much easier to debug when traces/run.jsonl doesn't look right, since each seam's writer is self-contained.

hooks.register's actual signature and whether hooks must return ctx. I'm assuming (based on the pattern elsewhere in your pipeline like hooks.run(hooks.AFTER_OCR, {"chunks": text})) that hook functions receive and pass through a ctx dict, but double-check whether hooks.py expects a return value at all, or just calls the function for its side effect.

Since agent/agent.py doesn't exist yet (that's your A3 stage), you won't be able to fully test this against real ON_STEP/ON_TOOL_CALL firing until that's built — but implementing it now means it's ready to go once the agent loop exists, and it's a self-contained, low-risk piece to knock out while your partner or you are between other A2 tasks.

class TraceStep(BaseModel):
    """One agent step, emitted to traces/run.jsonl so the A3 agentic-feature check can read the path."""
    step: int
    tool: str                 # tool name, or "decide" / "answer"
    args: dict                # e.g. {"query": "..."} — for retrieve, the query actually used
    obs: dict                 # what decide() saw, e.g. {"top_score": 0.31, "n": 10}

Good — now with the real schema, here's the correct implementation:

"""FIXED — structured logging (auditable NFR). Use get_logger(), never print()."""
from __future__ import annotations
import logging
import sys
import itertools
from pathlib import Path

from .contracts import TraceStep  # adjust import path to match where contracts.py actually lives

def get_logger(name: str) -> logging.Logger:
    lg = logging.getLogger(name)
    if not lg.handlers:
        h = logging.StreamHandler(sys.stdout)
        h.setFormatter(logging.Formatter(
            '{"ts":"%(asctime)s","lvl":"%(levelname)s","mod":"%(name)s","msg":"%(message)s"}'
        ))
        lg.addHandler(h)
        lg.setLevel(logging.INFO)
    return lg

_TRACE_PATH = Path("traces/run.jsonl")
_step_counter = itertools.count(1)  # monotonic step id across one process run

def _write_trace_step(step: TraceStep) -> None:
    _TRACE_PATH.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    with open(_TRACE_PATH, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write(step.model_dump_json() + "\n")

def register(hooks) -> None:
    """Wire structured tracing at each seam (auditable trail) AND emit traces/run.jsonl.
    Each seam appends a contracts.TraceStep line to traces/run.jsonl so the A3 agentic-feature
    check can read the trajectory (path must depend on observations)."""
    logger = get_logger("trace")

    def _trace_step(ctx: dict) -> dict:
        # ON_STEP fires once per agent decision -- tool="decide", args=whatever the
        # agent chose to do next, obs=what decide() saw when making that choice
        # (e.g. retrieval scores) -- this is the "path depends on observations" signal.
        step = TraceStep(
            step=next(_step_counter),
            tool="decide",
            args=ctx.get("args", ctx.get("action", {})),
            obs=ctx.get("obs", ctx.get("observation", {})),
        )
        _write_trace_step(step)
        logger.info(f"trace step {step.step}: tool=decide")
        return ctx

    def _trace_tool_call(ctx: dict) -> dict:
        # ON_TOOL_CALL fires once per actual tool invocation (e.g. retrieve) --
        # tool = real tool name, args = the actual args used (e.g. the query
        # actually sent to retrieval), obs = what came back / was observed after.
        step = TraceStep(
            step=next(_step_counter),
            tool=ctx.get("tool", ctx.get("tool_name", "unknown_tool")),
            args=ctx.get("args", {}),
            obs=ctx.get("obs", ctx.get("result", {})),
        )
        _write_trace_step(step)
        logger.info(f"trace step {step.step}: tool={step.tool}")
        return ctx

    def _trace_answer(ctx: dict) -> dict:
        # AFTER_ANSWER -- final step, tool="answer"
        step = TraceStep(
            step=next(_step_counter),
            tool="answer",
            args=ctx.get("args", {}),
            obs=ctx.get("obs", {"answer": ctx.get("answer")}),
        )
        _write_trace_step(step)
        logger.info(f"trace step {step.step}: tool=answer")
        return ctx

    hooks.register(hooks.ON_STEP, _trace_step)
    hooks.register(hooks.ON_TOOL_CALL, _trace_tool_call)
    hooks.register(hooks.AFTER_ANSWER, _trace_answer)

Things to verify once agent/agent.py exists and you can actually fire these hooks:

What keys ctx really carries at each seam. I'm guessing at ctx.get("args", ...) / ctx.get("obs", ...) fallbacks since I don't have agent.py or hooks.py in front of me — once the agent loop is written, check what dict shape it actually passes into hooks.run(hooks.ON_STEP, {...}) etc., and tighten these to read the real keys directly instead of guessing with fallbacks.

args and obs must genuinely be dicts (not strings or other types) — TraceStep declares both as dict, so if ctx["obs"] ever comes through as e.g. a raw float or string, wrap it ({"value": ctx["obs"]}) before constructing TraceStep, or pydantic validation will raise at trace time instead of silently logging bad data — which is actually the right failure mode for an audit trail (better to crash loudly than log something wrong), but good to know if you hit it.

The step field should probably reset per query/run, not stay monotonic across the whole process lifetime, depending on what "the path" means for grading — if the grader expects step to restart at 1 for each answer() call, reset _step_counter at the top of agent.Agent.run() rather than module-load time. Worth checking the grading rubric's exact expectation for what a single "trajectory" is scoped to.

This is a good one to leave implemented and tested with a tiny fake hooks/ctx stub now (even before the real agent exists), just to confirm traces/run.jsonl writes valid JSONL — cheap insurance before A3 depends on it.

"""FIXED — cross-cutting seam. Horizontal features register handlers here; the pipeline and agent
call run() at fixed seams. Do NOT add/remove seams or the hooks.run() calls that use them."""
from future import annotations
from collections import defaultdict
from typing import Callable

The only points where cross-cutting code runs.

AFTER_INGEST = "after_ingest"
AFTER_OCR = "after_ocr"
BEFORE_INDEX = "before_index"
AFTER_RETRIEVE = "after_retrieve"
ON_STEP = "on_step"
ON_TOOL_CALL = "on_tool_call"
BEFORE_ANSWER = "before_answer"
AFTER_ANSWER = "after_answer"
ON_LOG = "on_log"
SEAMS = [AFTER_INGEST, AFTER_OCR, BEFORE_INDEX, AFTER_RETRIEVE, ON_STEP,
         ON_TOOL_CALL, BEFORE_ANSWER, AFTER_ANSWER, ON_LOG]

_handlers: dict[str, list[Callable]] = defaultdict(list)

def register(seam: str, handler: Callable) -> None:
"""Attach a handler to a seam. Called by each feature's register() via wiring.py."""
assert seam in SEAMS, f"unknown seam {seam}"
    _handlers[seam].append(handler)

def run(seam: str, ctx: dict) -> dict:
"""Run every handler registered at seam, threading ctx through. Called at fixed points only."""
for h in _handlers[seam]:
        ctx = h(ctx) or ctx
return ctx

def clear() -> None:
    _handlers.clear()

Do you need to use this in A2?

Yes, but mostly passively — you're not writing new code against hooks.py directly for A2, you're relying on two things already being wired correctly:

1. Your pipeline.py already calls the three A2-relevant seams — hooks.run(hooks.AFTER_INGEST, {"pages": pages}), hooks.run(hooks.AFTER_OCR, {"chunks": text}), hooks.run(hooks.BEFORE_INDEX, {"chunks": chunks}). As long as those calls stay exactly where they are (per the "do not reorder" instruction), any horizontal feature that registers a handler at those seams — like logging_conf.py's tracing, or PII redaction mentioned in the AFTER_OCR comment — fires automatically without your pipeline code needing to know it exists. That's the whole point of this cross-cutting design: pipeline.py stays clean, features opt in via register().

2. wiring.register_all(cfg) needs to actually call each feature's register(hooks). This is the piece you haven't shown me yet — wiring.py is presumably a small file that does something like:

def register_all(cfg: dict) -> None:
    from . import logging_conf
    logging_conf.register(hooks)
    # ... any other cross-cutting features (PII redaction, cost tracking, etc.)

If wiring.py doesn't call logging_conf.register(hooks), then the tracing we just implemented never actually attaches to AFTER_INGEST/AFTER_OCR/BEFORE_INDEX — the seams fire, but with zero handlers registered, run()'s for h in _handlers[seam] loop just does nothing. Worth checking that file next; if it's also a stub, that's the actual missing piece connecting everything.

The ON_STEP, ON_TOOL_CALL, BEFORE_ANSWER, AFTER_ANSWER seams are A3-only — they only fire inside agent.Agent.run(), which doesn't exist yet. So logging_conf.py's tracing on those three won't produce any traces/run.jsonl entries until A3's agent loop is built and calls hooks.run() at those points itself. Nothing to do about that now — just don't expect trace output yet from those three seams during A2 testing.

Practical next step: paste wiring.py if you want me to confirm it's correctly calling logging_conf.register(hooks) (and whatever else needs registering) — that's the one remaining link between "the code we wrote" and "it actually runs during your A2 pipeline."

"""Stage 4 — vector store"""
from future import annotations
from ..contracts import *  # noqa
import json
from pathlib import Path
import numpy as np
import faiss

def _index_dir(cfg: dict) -> Path:
    data_root = Path(cfg.get("ingest", {}).get("data_root", "data"))
    index_cfg = cfg.get("index", {})
return data_root / index_cfg.get("index_dir", "interim/index")

def build(chunks: list[Chunk], vectors: np.ndarray, cfg: dict) -> None:
    out_dir = _index_dir(cfg)
    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

dim = vectors.shape[1]

index.add(vectors)

Architected incremental caching strategy using chunk IDs and metadata mapping.

Architected incremental caching strategy using chunk IDs and metadata mapping.

Done

Here's store.py with per-chunk caching — it loads any existing index/metadata, figures out which chunk IDs are already indexed, and only adds vectors for chunks that are missing:

"""Stage 4 — vector store"""
from __future__ import annotations
from ..contracts import *  # noqa
import json
from pathlib import Path
import numpy as np
import faiss

def _index_dir(cfg: dict) -> Path:
    data_root = Path(cfg.get("ingest", {}).get("data_root", "data"))
    index_cfg = cfg.get("index", {})
    return data_root / index_cfg.get("index_dir", "interim/index")

def _index_path(out_dir: Path) -> Path:
    return out_dir / "index.faiss"

def _meta_path(out_dir: Path) -> Path:
    return out_dir / "chunks.jsonl"

def _load_existing(out_dir: Path) -> tuple[faiss.Index | None, list[dict]]:
    """Load a previously-built index + its chunk metadata, if present.
    Returns (None, []) if nothing has been built yet."""
    index_path = _index_path(out_dir)
    meta_path = _meta_path(out_dir)
    if not (index_path.exists() and meta_path.exists()):
        return None, []

    index = faiss.read_index(str(index_path))
    meta: list[dict] = []
    with open(meta_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        for line in f:
            line = line.strip()
            if line:
                meta.append(json.loads(line))
    return index, meta

def build(chunks: list[Chunk], vectors: np.ndarray, cfg: dict) -> None:
    out_dir = _index_dir(cfg)
    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    if vectors.shape[0] != len(chunks):
        raise ValueError(
            f"vectors/chunks length mismatch: {vectors.shape[0]} vectors vs {len(chunks)} chunks -- "
            "these must stay aligned (vectors[i] corresponds to chunks[i])."
        )

    existing_index, existing_meta = _load_existing(out_dir)
    existing_ids = {row["id"] for row in existing_meta}

    # Split incoming chunks into "already indexed" vs "missing" by id.
    missing_positions = [i for i, c in enumerate(chunks) if c.id not in existing_ids]

    if existing_index is not None and not missing_positions:
        print(f"[store] cache hit: all {len(chunks)} chunks already indexed "
              f"({existing_index.ntotal} vectors in {out_dir}) -- nothing to build")
        return

    dim = vectors.shape[1]

    if existing_index is None:
        # Nothing cached yet -- build fresh, exactly as before.
        index = faiss.IndexFlatIP(dim)
        new_vectors = vectors
        new_chunks = chunks
        print(f"[store] no existing index found -- building fresh from {len(chunks)} chunks")
    else:
        if existing_index.d != dim:
            raise ValueError(
                f"cached index dim ({existing_index.d}) != incoming vector dim ({dim}) -- "
                "embedding model/config likely changed; delete the cached index to rebuild from scratch."
            )
        index = existing_index
        new_vectors = vectors[missing_positions]
        new_chunks = [chunks[i] for i in missing_positions]
        print(f"[store] cache partial hit: {len(existing_ids)} chunks already indexed, "
              f"adding {len(new_chunks)} missing chunk(s)")

    if len(new_chunks) > 0:
        index.add(new_vectors)

    faiss.write_index(index, str(_index_path(out_dir)))

    # Append only the newly-added chunk metadata, in the SAME order they were
    # just added to the index, so row order in chunks.jsonl still matches
    # the index's internal vector order (existing rows are untouched, new
    # rows are appended after them -- consistent with FAISS's append-only add()).
    if len(new_chunks) > 0:
        with open(_meta_path(out_dir), "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            for c in new_chunks:
                f.write(json.dumps(c.model_dump(), ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")

    print(f"[store] index now has {index.ntotal} vectors "
          f"({len(new_chunks)} newly added) + matching chunk records in {out_dir}")

Key design points, worth double-checking against your actual pipeline:

Caching key is chunk.id — same deterministic id scheme ocr.py already uses (f"{region.page_id}:{x1}-{y1}-{x2}-{y2}"), so this works cleanly as long as chunk IDs stay stable across pipeline runs on unchanged content, which they should given how _region_chunk_id() is built.

Vectors must still be computed for all chunks upstream — this caching is scoped to store.build() only, per your ask. It skips the FAISS-add + write cost for already-indexed chunks, but embed.encode() will still run over the full chunk list before this function ever sees it (unless you also want caching one stage earlier, in embed.py — say if that's useful, and I can add ID-based skip logic there too, since re-embedding unchanged chunks is likely the more expensive step to actually cache).

Append-only semantics — faiss.IndexFlatIP doesn't support efficient deletion/update by id, so this design only adds missing chunks; it won't detect or fix a chunk whose content changed while its id stayed the same (that shouldn't happen given how ids are derived from bbox/page, but worth knowing as a limitation).

Dimension-mismatch guard — if you ever swap embedding models (e.g. testing BGE-M3 vs something else), this will raise a clear error instead of silently corrupting the index; the fix is just deleting interim/index/ to force a rebuild.

If re-embedding cost is actually your bottleneck (skipping FAISS writes alone probably won't save much time relative to OCR+embedding), let me know and I'll add the same id-based skip to embed.py so unchanged chunks skip the model call entirely, not just the index write.

Add this new function (near _get_corrector):

import kagglehub

def _resolve_corrector_checkpoint(cfg: dict) -> str:
    """Download the mT5 corrector checkpoint from the private Kaggle dataset
    if it isn't already cached locally, and return the local path to it."""
    ocr_cfg = cfg.get("ocr", {})
    local_override = ocr_cfg.get("corrector_checkpoint_path")

    # If it's already a real local directory (e.g. you're testing with a
    # manually-placed copy), use it directly -- skip the download.
    if local_override and Path(local_override).is_dir():
        return local_override

    kaggle_dataset = ocr_cfg.get("corrector_kaggle_dataset", "haha34/mt5-small-2200")
    print(f"[ocr] downloading corrector checkpoint from Kaggle dataset: {kaggle_dataset}")
    local_path = kagglehub.dataset_download(kaggle_dataset)
    print(f"[ocr] checkpoint cached at: {local_path}")
    return local_path

Config change — since it's a private dataset, kagglehub needs your Kaggle credentials available. Two ways, pick one:

Env vars (simplest, works with uv run):
export KAGGLE_USERNAME=your_username
export KAGGLE_KEY=your_api_key

~/.kaggle/kaggle.json (the standard Kaggle CLI credentials file) — kagglehub will pick this up automatically if it exists, no env vars needed.

Either way, this must be set up on any machine that runs this code — your partner's machine too, or their run will fail at this exact download step with an auth error.

config.yaml — you can leave corrector_checkpoint_path as-is (it'll just be ignored/used as a local-cache check now), or add the new key explicitly:

ocr:
  corrector_kaggle_dataset: "haha34/mt5-small-2200"   # NEW
  # corrector_checkpoint_path: "mt5-small-2200"       # now optional -- only used if it's already a local dir

One thing worth confirming: kagglehub.dataset_download() caches downloads locally after the first pull (usually under ~/.cache/kagglehub/), so this only actually hits the network once per machine, not on every run — good for your teammate's repeated run_ingest.py invocations. Also double check model.safetensors/config.json/tokenizer.json sit at the root of what dataset_download() returns (based on your screenshot's file list, they do) — AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(checkpoint_path) and AutoModelForSeq2SeqLM.from_pretrained(checkpoint_path, ...) expect exactly that flat layout, not a nested subfolder.

8/15/2026 19:47:26

Yes — this is exactly the case logging_conf.py's docstring was calling out. Everything you're seeing here ([timing], [ocr], [store], [chunk], the region-accounting block) is structured pipeline status, which is precisely what an audit trail is for. Right now it's all raw print(), which means:

No log level distinction — a [timing] line and an actual error would look identical in output.

Not machine-parseable — if a grader (or your own eval script) wants to programmatically check "did this run actually process N pages," they have to regex your print statements instead of reading structured JSON.

Doesn't integrate with the get_logger() convention already established in logging_conf.py, which explicitly says "Use get_logger(), never print()."

What I'd convert, and what I'd leave alone:

Convert to logger.info(...):

[timing] tesseract pass..., [timing] mT5 corrector loaded..., [timing] correction:...

[ocr] sent N/M lines to corrector...

[store] cache hit... / [store] index now has...

[chunk] N pages -> M chunks...

The === Region accounting === block and === Tesseract (raw/corrected) === metrics blocks

Leave as tqdm progress bars (tesseract: 100%|...) — those are interactive terminal UI, not audit-trail content; converting them to logging would just be noisy in a log file.

Practical approach — minimal-diff swap:

At the top of ocr.py, chunk.py, embed.py, store.py:

from .logging_conf import get_logger  # adjust relative import path as needed
logger = get_logger(__name__)

Then replace each print(f"...") with logger.info(f"...") — same f-string content, just swap the call. For the multi-line _MetricAccumulator.report() method specifically, since it prints several related lines, either keep it as sequential logger.info() calls or bundle it into one structured line:

logger.info(f"{label}: corpus cer={corpus[0]:.4f} wer={corpus[1]:.4f} n={len(self.all_ref)}; "
            f"held-out {'cer=%.4f wer=%.4f n=%d' % (held[0], held[1], len(self.held_ref)) if held else 'skipped'}")

One thing to decide: get_logger()'s current formatter wraps everything in a JSON-ish string ('{"ts":"...","lvl":"...","mod":"...","msg":"..."}'), but the %(message)s slot will just contain your raw f-string text, not further-structured fields (e.g. n_pages, cer as separate JSON keys). That's fine for human/grep readability, but if you want the audit trail to be properly machine-parseable (e.g. so a script could extract CER trends over time), you'd want to pass structured extra={} fields into the logger call instead of baking everything into one message string. For A2, plain logger.info(f"...") swapped in for print() is a reasonable, low-effort win — I wouldn't over-engineer this further unless the grading rubric specifically asks for structured fields beyond the message string.
