Section 2, Field 2 — "Structure in the text"

Text is a 1D sequence where order encodes meaning, and sub-word units combine compositionally — a sequence model can exploit this directly. (1) Sequential & orthographic structure: reading order is strictly left-to-right, but conjunct clusters (যুক্তাক্ষর) and floating matras mean a single "character" is often a multi-glyph composition — the OCR/HTR stage must reason over character sequences, not isolated glyphs, and downstream chunking must preserve conjunct boundaries rather than splitting mid-cluster. (2) Domain vocabulary regularity: script composition is 94–97% Bengali letters per book, with recurring domain terms (soil prep, crop-rotation sequences, fertilizer/N-P-K ratios, Bengali numerals) that a dense embedder can use for contextual disambiguation of OCR-ambiguous tokens — the Zipf-shaped rank-frequency curve (Section 3) confirms natural-language regularity rather than noise, so retrieval can lean on real term frequency signal. (3) Structural divergence across books: GT-derived content flags show BKBP is the most heavily annotated/structured text (footnotes 52.8% of pages, bilingual terms 59.9%) and KB is moderately so (footnotes 22.8%, bilingual terms 58.5%), while KD is comparatively bare prose (footnotes 3.2%, bilingual terms 0.5%) — meaning chunking/embedding must handle both footnote-annotated, cross-referenced text (KB/BKBP) and continuous narrative with fewer structural anchors (KD), rather than assuming one document shape corpus-wide.

Section 2, Field 3 — "What should NOT change the answer"

The pipeline's output — retrieved evidence and the final agricultural answer — must stay invariant to variation that is purely visual/physical, not semantic. (1) Scan geometry & surface artifacts: minor page skew/rotation, binding shadows near the spine, paper yellowing/staining, and ink bleed density from 1930s–40s letterpress printing must not alter retrieval or answer content — our measured skew is already small and consistent (mean skew_angle_deg: BKBP −0.03°, KB 0.03°, KD −0.04°), but the pipeline should tolerate a wider real-world range (~±5°) than what this sample happens to show. (2) Per-book degradation severity: the system must produce equivalent answer quality despite large swings in raw image quality — blur_score varies more than 2.5× across books (BKBP 3,454.72 vs KD 9,086.48), and contrast_std varies substantially too (KD 53.60, thin/broken strokes needing closing operations, vs BKBP 68.73, dark/bleedy ink) — so enhancement/OCR confidence handling must normalize for this rather than treating one book's scan profile as the default. (3) Non-semantic layout & typography: running headers, page numbers, marginal decoration, and minor font-size/type differences across the three distinct pre-independence printings must not be treated as content — layout detection should segment these out before OCR/chunking so they never enter the retrieval index as if they were agricultural knowledge.

That completes Section 2. Let me know if you'd like any edits, or if you want to move on to Section 3.

Context Summary for the Second AI
Here is a summary of the underlying codebase implementation across Stages 1–3 to serve as the ground truth context:

Ingest & Enhancement (Stage 1):

Loader (loader.py): Renders raw document PDFs from data/raw into 150 DPI PNG page images (pdf2image).

Enhancer (enhance.py): Performs connected-components analysis (cv2.connectedComponentsWithStats) following Otsu binarization and morphological opening (cv2.MORPH_OPEN with a $2\times2$ kernel). Filters blank pages using min_blob_area = 15, ink_ratio_threshold = 0.002, and min_blob_count = 3.

Layout Detection (Stage 2):

Model (vision/layout.py): Utilizes DocLayout-YOLO (doclayout_yolo_docstructbench_imgsz1024.pt via HuggingFace) operating at imgsz = 1184 and score_thr = 0.085.

Post-Processing: Maps fine-grained DocStructBench classes to 4 unified region kinds (heading, text, table, figure). Applies class-agnostic deduplication via non-maximum suppression (dedup_iou_thr = 0.5) followed by dropping nested boxes (containment_thr = 0.85).

OCR / HTR (Stage 3):

Engine (vision/ocr.py): Two-stage hybrid pipeline. Stage 1 uses Tesseract OCR (pytesseract, language ben, psm=6 for body text/tables and psm=7 for single-line headings). Stage 2 applies a Seq2Seq mT5 corrector model fine-tuned for OCR error correction (ocr_fix: prompt, batched inference, beam search num_beams=1). Figures are skipped. Evaluation is measured via character error rate (CER) and word error rate (WER) against ground truth (jiwer).# Section 3 Task Instructions: Enhancement & Layout Detection

Context Summary of Stages 3–5 for the Second LLM
Here is a technical summary of the code across Stages 3, 4, and 5 (ocr.py, chunk.py, embed.py, and store.py) to serve as the ground truth context:

Stage 3 — OCR / HTR (ocr.py):
Pipeline: Two-pass hybrid approach. Pass 1 runs Tesseract OCR (pytesseract, language ben, psm=6 for body text/tables, psm=7 for single-line headings). Pass 2 applies a batched mT5 Seq2Seq corrector model (ocr_fix: prompt, num_beams=1) fine-tuned for OCR error correction on Bangla text. Figures are skipped.

Evaluation: Computes corpus-wide and held-out Character Error Rate (CER) and Word Error Rate (WER) using jiwer against ground truth text files.

Stage 4 — Chunking (chunk.py):
Strategy: Dynamic region-aware merging and sentence-based fallback splitting using the BAAI/bge-m3 tokenizer.

Logic:
Merge: Non-heading page regions (sorted spatially by $y$-then-$x$ bounding box coordinates) are greedily packed up to target_tokens = 256. Tables are strictly kept as standalone chunks to prevent structural dilution.

Split: If a merged chunk exceeds max_tokens = 512, it is split by sentences (delimited by Bangla dandi ।) with overlap_tokens = 32 carried over.

Stage 5 — Embedding (embed.py):
Model: Uses BAAI/bge-m3 (SentenceTransformer).

Features: Generates dense 1024-dimensional embeddings with normalize_embeddings=True to enable cosine similarity via dot product.

Stage 6 — Vector Store (store.py):
Index: Implements FAISS with an exact inner-product index (faiss.IndexFlatIP).

Storage: Stores index to index.faiss and maps retrieved vector row indices directly to metadata in chunks.jsonl.

Section 3 Task Instructions: Components & Options We Considered (Remaining Rows)

We are continuing Section 3 — Components & Options We Considered of A2_form.docx. We need to draft the final four table rows: OCR / HTR, Chunking, Embedding, and Vector Index.

Ground Truth Context from Codebase (src/doc_agent/):

OCR / HTR (ocr.py):
Options compared: Pure Tesseract OCR vs. Vision-LLM/TrOCR end-to-end vs. Hybrid two-stage pipeline (Tesseract OCR + fine-tuned Seq2Seq LLM corrector).

Our choice & rationale: Two-stage pipeline. Stage 1 uses Tesseract (pytesseract, language ben, psm=6 for body text/tables, psm=7 for headings). Stage 2 uses a batched fine-tuned mT5 corrector (ocr_fix: prompt, num_beams=1) to fix systematic OCR errors in sequence space. Figures are skipped. Evaluated via jiwer (CER/WER) against ground truth. Chosen because pure Tesseract struggles with 1930s–40s pre-reform Bangla conjuncts (যুক্তাক্ষর) and floating matras, while mT5 corrects errors efficiently without vision re-training.

Reproduced?: Yes — Fine-tuned and adapted a pretrained foundation Seq2Seq model (mT5) for sequence correction.

Chunking (chunk.py):
Options compared: Fixed character/word sliding window (with overlap) vs. Structural layout region chunking vs. Hybrid region-aware merging with sentence-splitting fallback.

Our choice & rationale: Hybrid region-aware merging (target_tokens=256, max_tokens=512, overlap=32 using BAAI/bge-m3 tokenizer). Spatial regions on a page are greedily packed up to target length. Tables are strictly isolated as standalone chunks. Oversized regions are split along Bangla sentence boundaries (।). Chosen because fixed windows cut conjuncts/words mid-token and break structured crop yield tables, while naive region chunking yields fragmented single-line chunks.

Reproduced?: No.

Embedding (embed.py):
Options compared: Monolingual/English-centric dense embedders vs. Multilingual dense embedders (e.g., BAAI/bge-m3, text-embedding-3-small, multilingual-e5-base).

Our choice & rationale: BAAI/bge-m3 with normalize_embeddings=True. Chosen for its strong native multilingual/low-resource language representation (specifically Bangla), 1024-dimensional dense semantic capture, and native support for normalized cosine similarity computations.

Reproduced?: Yes — Loaded and deployed the pretrained BAAI/bge-m3 embedding model.

Vector Index (store.py):
Options compared: Managed/cloud vector databases (e.g., Qdrant, Pinecone) vs. Local approximate nearest neighbor (ANN) indexes (e.g., HNSW) vs. Exact local vector index (FAISS IndexFlatIP).

Our choice & rationale: FAISS IndexFlatIP storing normalized embeddings with aligned metadata (chunks.jsonl). Chosen because our corpus total scale (749 pages across 3 books) generates a moderate chunk count (a few thousand chunks) where exact inner-product search ($O(N)$) executes in sub-millisecond latent time on CPU with zero recall loss, avoiding quantization distortion or vector DB server overhead.

Reproduced?: No.

The pipeline in one line:

pages → clean (Otsu + morphological blob filtering) → layout (DocLayout-YOLO, 4-class collapse) → OCR (Tesseract) → mT5 error-correction pass → chunk (region-aware merge, tables isolated, sentence-boundary fallback) → embed (bge-m3) → store (FAISS IndexFlatIP). Changed from a naive default pipeline in three places: added the mT5 corrector as a second OCR pass instead of relying on raw Tesseract output; added class-agnostic NMS + containment filtering on top of DocLayout-YOLO's raw boxes to remove duplicate/nested detections; and strictly isolated table regions as standalone chunks rather than letting them merge into surrounding prose text.

A short reflection:
What I Accepted:
DocLayout-YOLO for Page Segmentation: Accepted doclayout_yolo as the primary layout detector because it handles multi-class region proposals natively and runs smoothly on PyTorch/Hugging Face without OS-level build dependencies.
Filtering Figure Regions Pre-OCR: Accepted ignoring kind == "figure" regions during Tesseract OCR passes in vision/ocr.py, preventing non-text visual diagrams from turning into gibberish text in vector indices.
Hybrid Sparse-Dense Retrieval Strategy: Accepted combining BM25 keyword matching with dense vector embeddings to ensure complex technical terms and Bangla script variants are accurately retrieved.
What I Changed:
Pivot from Heuristic/OpenCV Layout Detection to Deep Learning: Shifted from OpenCV connected-component logic to DocLayout-YOLO. Why: Classical thresholding failed on Bangla script because the continuous matra line joined adjacent characters into single large bounding boxes misidentified as figures.
Simplification of Post-Processing Pipeline: Reverted custom box-merging logic in favor of raw DocLayout-YOLO outputs after visual inspection of 20+ debug images (visualize_regions) showed post-processing patches caused bounding-box regressions.
Text Chunking Boundary Strategy: Adjusted chunking logic to align with detected structural headings (kind == "heading") rather than fixed character counts, preserving semantic integrity across document sections.
What I Rejected:
Detectron2 / PubLayNet Framework: Rejected early during setup due to recurring dependency conflicts, Dropbox cache errors, and compilation hurdles across different development OS environments.
OCR Confidence as a Layout Classifier: Rejected using OCR failure rates to distinguish text from images, as degraded historical text (a core requirement of the dataset) would be falsely dropped as "figures."
Inter-stage OCR Result Caching: Rejected using image_to_data JSON caches between layout and OCR stages because image_to_data reduced overall text quality and stripped formatting compared to direct image_to_string extraction.
